Because the result is then used as the geography filter key at line 163, the mislabelled company is not merely tagged wrongly — it is removed from the run.

**Failure.** Verified: a company with 39 listings located 'Berlin, Germany' (no stated code) plus 1 listing with country='US' from a remote search is labelled US by country_of and, with countries=frozenset({'DE','AT','CH'}), companies_from_listings returns []. A large Berlin employer disappears from a German run because of one remote posting.

**Fix.** Count stated and inferred codes into one tally with stated votes weighted higher (e.g. 3:1) rather than as an absolute override, and break ties deterministically by the largest weighted count. Alternatively require the stated set to have at least as many votes as the inferred mode before it wins.

### jobs.ch knows the filter's total result count and never warns when max_pages truncates the walk

`src/leadgen/seed/jobsch.py:183` · silent-failure

**What happens.** `_uuids` extracts the board's own result count on page 1 and logs it, then walks up to `max_pages` pages and returns whatever it collected. It never compares the two. `fetch` then logs '%d postings matched the filter' using `len(uuids)`, which is the *harvested* count, not the matched count - so a truncated walk reports itself as a complete one. The default `max_pages=40` in the `fetch` signature and the shipped `PAGES=65` in daily.sh are both below what the live filter currently needs.

**Failure.** Operator raises `--jobsch-days 30` after a holiday; the filter now matches 1,469 jobs but `--jobsch-pages 40` stops at 880. The log says '880 postings matched the filter' next to 'filter reports 1469 matching jobs', and nothing flags the 589 missing postings as a truncation the operator can fix by raising the page count.

**Fix.** Parse the reported total into an int, and when `len(found) < total` after the loop exits on `max_pages`, log a warning naming both numbers and the flag that would raise the cap. Fix the misleading wording at jobsch.py:279.

### jobs.ch cannot tell a layout change or a 200-served bot wall from an empty filter - both yield a silent zero-posting seed

`src/leadgen/seed/jobsch.py:187` · silent-failure

**What happens.** `_uuids` relies entirely on `DETAIL_RX` matching `/en/vacancies/detail/<36-char uuid>` in the SSR HTML. If jobs.ch changes its detail URL shape, renders the cards client-side, or serves a 200-status interstitial, the regex finds nothing, `if not new: break` fires on page 1, and `fetch` returns `[]` after logging '0 postings matched the filter'. That is the identical output to a filter that genuinely matches nothing. The evidence needed to tell them apart is in hand and ignored: `_RESULT_COUNT_RX` reports the board's own count, and a page that reports 1,469 matches while yielding 0 uuids is a parser failure, not an empty filter. The same `break` also swallows a mid-walk 403/429 (`if not response: ... break`), silently truncating the walk.

**Failure.** jobs.ch ships a redesign. The Swiss daily run logs 'jobs.ch: filter reports 1469 matching jobs' immediately followed by 'jobs.ch: 0 postings matched the filter', exits 2 with a SHORTFALL, and the operator reads it as 'nobody is hiring' rather than 'the parser is dead'.

**Fix.** When page 1 yields zero uuids but `_RESULT_COUNT_RX` reports a non-zero total, raise or log an ERROR naming a layout change. Distinguish `response is None` (blocked/unreachable) from 'parsed but empty' in the break, and warn on the former.

### --search-delay never fires on the request pattern that caused the block, because the sleep sits behind the nextPageToken check

`src/leadgen/seed/jobsearch.py:134` · anti-bot

**What happens.** The pagination loop sleeps only after confirming there is a next page: `token = payload.get("nextPageToken") or ""` / `if not token: break` / `if delay_seconds: sleep(delay_seconds)`. Any (country, keyword) pairing whose result set fits in one page - the large majority - therefore issues its request with zero added delay, and the loop exits before the sleep is ever reached. Since the dominant cost of a Europe run is one page-1 request per pairing, `--search-delay` (the flag added specifically because 'This host blocked a whole run') does essentially nothing to the traffic shape that caused the block.

**Failure.** `./scripts/daily.sh` with REGION=europe passes `--search-delay 0.4`. For Estonia, 31 of 34 non-empty keyword pairings are single-page: 31 requests fire back to back with zero of the intended 0.4s spacing. The operator believes the run is paced; it is not.

**Fix.** Move the delay to the top of the loop body (or apply it per pairing in the outer loop), so every request is paced regardless of whether a next page exists.

### A blocked query and a genuinely empty query are collapsed into the same state, discarding the distinguishing evidence the response already carries

`src/leadgen/seed/jobsearch.py:124` · silent-failure

**What happens.** `_json` returns None for a 403, a 429, a robots refusal, a network error and a non-JSON body alike, because `HttpClient.get` collapses all of those to None. `search_workable` then does `if not isinstance(payload, dict): break` (blocked) and `if not jobs: break` (genuinely empty) - and both land on `pages == 0`, which is the only thing `empty` counts. The information needed to tell them apart is right there and is thrown away: a real empty answer is a 200 with `{"totalSize": 0, "jobs": []}`, whereas a block yields no payload at all. The run therefore cannot report 'Workable blocked us on 400 queries' versus 'those 400 vocabulary/geography pairings have no postings'.

**Failure.** Workable starts 429-ing at request ~1,500 of a Europe sweep. Every subsequent pairing yields `payload=None`, counted identically to Iceland's honest zero. The funnel prints 'seed: N listings total' with a much smaller N and the operator has no way to tell a block from a thin vocabulary.

**Fix.** Count three outcomes separately - blocked (payload is None), empty (dict with totalSize 0), and productive - and log all three. Escalate to a warning (or abort the sweep) as soon as the blocked count crosses a threshold, rather than inferring a block from emptiness after the fact.

### The shipped Europe run issues more page-1 search requests than the number the code itself documents as the block threshold

`src/leadgen/seed/jobsearch.py:106` · anti-bot

**What happens.** `search_workable`'s own docstring records that jobs.workable.com 'answered ~1,500 requests and then refused everything from this IP for the best part of an hour'. The shipped configuration exceeds that on page 1 alone, before any `--search-max-pages` multiplication: `scripts/daily.sh` with REGION=europe expands `--countries europe` to `search_names(EU_COUNTRIES | EFTA_AND_UK)` = 32 location names, paired with all 52 lines of configs/leads/keywords.txt = 1,664 pairings, each costing at least one request. The runbook's full-run command (`--countries eu`, 27 names) is 1,404 plus multi-page extras. With `CITIES=1` it becomes 96 x 52 = 4,992.

**Failure.** A REGION=europe daily run gets through roughly the first 20 countries alphabetically, then Workable starts refusing. Countries from Poland onward return nothing, and because of the finding above that reads as 'those countries have no postings'. The next morning's run repeats the same order and starves the same countries.

**Fix.** Bound the sweep by request budget rather than by list cross-product: stop paginating a pairing once `totalSize` is exhausted (it is returned on page 1), drop the city list (see the subset finding), and add a hard per-host request ceiling that aborts the seed with a clear message instead of degrading into silent emptiness.

### The test that claims to validate the runner scripts' command lines is a hand-transcribed copy that never reads the scripts

`tests/leadgen/test_daily.py:179` · coverage

**What happens.** tests/leadgen/test_daily.py:179-182 introduces the section with "The runner scripts build a command line the CLI has to accept. A typo there is invisible until somebody runs the harvest and it dies on argv." The test that follows (`test_every_flag_a_runner_passes_is_one_the_cli_accepts`, :247-253) parses two hardcoded Python lists, `CH_REGION_ARGS` (:184-212) and `EUROPE_REGION_ARGS` (:214-244). Neither list is derived from scripts/daily.sh or scripts/daily.ps1 — nothing in the file opens either script. The lists are already out of sync with what the scripts produce: `CH_REGION_ARGS` includes `--jobsch-term engineer` and `--register`, which daily.sh only appends conditionally (lines 122 and 139); `EUROPE_REGION_ARGS` includes `--search-locations`, which daily.sh only appends when `CITIES=1` (line 135); and neither list includes `--jobsch-url`, which daily.sh:137 can append.

**Failure.** Rename `--search-max-pages` to `--search-pages` in src/leadgen/cli.py:125 and update daily.sh:130 to match, but forget daily.ps1:144. Or introduce a typo into daily.ps1's flag list. In every such case this test still passes, because it is asserting against a third copy of the argv that neither script reads. The docstring's stated purpose — catching a typo in the runner scripts — is unachievable by construction.

**Fix.** Derive the argv from the scripts: run `bash scripts/daily.sh` with a stubbed `$PYTHON` that echoes its argv and exits 0 (and the PowerShell equivalent, skipped where pwsh is absent), then feed the captured argv to `build_parser().parse_args`. That test would fail on a real typo.

### The only test of the WAF block detector asserts that long challenge pages are NOT blocks, pinning the bug as intended behaviour

`tests/test_extract.py:177` · anti-bot

**What happens.** `_looks_like_block` is the sole bot-wall detector on the live path (http.py:590). Its entire test coverage is five assertions in tests/test_extract.py:177-182, all against synthetic strings. The last one is `assert _looks_like_block("<html>" + "content " * 2000 + "captcha</html>") is False` — it asserts that a 16 KB body containing "captcha" is a real page. That is a direct consequence of the `len(lo) < 5000` gate at http.py:80, and the test locks it in. Modern Cloudflare and DataDome interstitials are routinely 5-20 KB because of their inline challenge scripts, so the one shape that matters most in production is the shape this test blesses as fine. There is no test for a real 403 body, a real "Just a moment..." page, or the `"404 not found"` entry in `_BLOCK_KEYWORDS` (http.py:51) which misclassifies genuine 404 pages as blocks. Notably, the unused src/job_scraper/transport.py:185-208 implements this properly — 200,000-char scan, header signals, title matching — and it is the version with no tests at all.

**Failure.** A company domain returns HTTP 200 with a 9 KB Cloudflare JS challenge. `_looks_like_block` returns False, http.py:594-599 writes it to the SQLite cache, and with `cache_ttl_seconds: 604800` in configs/leads/eu-it.yaml:44 and swiss-it.yaml:17 it stays there for a week. The challenge HTML is handed to the person extractor, which finds nobody, and the funnel records `no_person_found`. Every re-run for seven days replays the cached challenge without a single request.

**Fix.** Replace the 5000-char gate with transport.py's `_body_is_challenge`, and add tests using captured real bodies: a Cloudflare interstitial, an Akamai 403 page, a DataDome challenge, and a genuine 404 that must NOT be classified as a block.


## MEDIUM

### CI's three quality gates cannot fail, there is no Windows runner, and config validation proves only that YAML parses

`.github/workflows/ci.yml:40` · coverage

**What happens.** Three of six CI jobs are incapable of failing. `type-check` (ci.yml:40) runs `mypy src/job_scraper/ || true`; `security-audit` (ci.yml:126) runs `pip-audit -r requirements.txt --ignore-vuln ... || true`; and the Makefile mirrors the first (Makefile:46 `mypy ... || true`), so `make check` — which README.md:366 presents as the developer gate — cannot fail on types either. `python-test` (ci.yml:56) collects coverage with `--cov=src --cov-report=xml` but sets no `--cov-fail-under`, so the 38% on http.py and 31% on robots.py never blocks anything. `config-validation` (ci.yml:75-93) loads every YAML and prints OK — it would happily pass `configs/leads/eu-it.yaml` with `targets: []`, which is the config that seeds nothing. No job runs on `windows-latest`, and nothing anywhere executes scripts/daily.ps1, scripts/setup.ps1, scripts/daily.sh, scripts/setup.sh or `docker build`. Lint (ci.yml:27-28) covers `src/ tests/ tools/ run.py` but omits `run_leads.py`, the lead product's entry point. Finally pyproject.toml:101-104 declares `integration` and `playwright` markers that zero tests use, so `--strict-markers` guards nothing and there is no live tier.

**Failure.** The product ships to Windows employee laptops (ONBOARDING.md:33-37, :74-79) and its two Windows scripts have never been executed by CI. The NativeCommandError abort under Windows PowerShell 5.1 documented above would have been caught by a single `windows-latest` job running `pwsh -File scripts/daily.ps1` and `powershell.exe -File scripts/daily.ps1` against a stub. Likewise a `--cov-fail-under=60` on http.py would have blocked the state where the request function has no test at all.

**Fix.** Drop the `|| true` from mypy and pip-audit (or move them to a clearly-labelled advisory job that is not called "Security"). Add `--cov-fail-under` with a floor that ratchets. Add a `windows-latest` job that shellchecks and dry-runs both PowerShell scripts under 5.1 and 7. Add `run_leads.py` to the lint paths. Either use the declared pytest markers or remove them.

### CHANGELOG is a product behind: wrong test count, wrong schema size, a config path that no longer exists, and no mention of leadgen

`CHANGELOG.md:15` · docs-mismatch

**What happens.** CHANGELOG.md:15 claims "Test suite grows 6 → 53 Python tests"; the suite is 453. Line 33 claims a "60-field shared schema" while README.md:278 and docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:3 both say 56 — the code says 56 (`len(JOB_FIELDS) == 56`, `len(CSV_COLUMNS) == 61`), so the CHANGELOG is the odd one out. Line 22 names "`config.norway-top5.yaml`", the pre-move root-level path; the file is `configs/regions/norway-top5.yaml`, and line 10 of the same document describes the move that renamed it. Line 11's strict-validation claim is false for three config sections (see the separate finding). Most significantly, the entire lead product — src/leadgen/, run_leads.py, the checkpoint and delivered ledger, scripts/daily.*, scripts/setup.*, docs/ONBOARDING.md, docs/OPERATOR-TERMS.md, the jobs.ch seed, the Zefix register integration — has no CHANGELOG entry at all, despite being what docs/ONBOARDING.md:1-8 calls "the whole job."

**Failure.** Anyone using the CHANGELOG to understand what shipped concludes the schema is 60 fields wide and writes an extension mapping for four columns that do not exist, or looks for config.norway-top5.yaml in the repo root. A reviewer checking "is this tested?" reads 53 and has no idea 400 more tests were added — or, more usefully, that they were all added to the parsing layers and none to the network layer.

**Fix.** Correct 53 -> 453, 60-field -> 56-field, and the norway-top5 path. Qualify the strict-validation entry to name the sections it actually covers. Add an Unreleased section for the leadgen product.

### README claims confirm_permission is required to run, but the lead pipeline never checks it

`README.md:330` · docs-mismatch

**What happens.** README.md:330, under "## Compliance", states: "`confirm_permission: true` (config) or `--confirm-permission` (CLI) required to run." That is enforced in src/job_scraper/main.py:45-47 and src/general_scraper/main.py:64-66. It is not enforced anywhere in src/leadgen — `grep -rn confirm_permission src/` returns no hit under that package. src/leadgen/cli.py:32-52 builds the HttpClient from `config.run` and reads user_agent, delay, robots, cache, rotation, throttle and proxy settings, but never `confirm_permission`. Both lead configs set it (configs/leads/eu-it.yaml:38, swiss-it.yaml:11) where it is inert.

**Failure.** The pipeline that crawls thousands of third-party company domains for personal data is the one with no permission gate, while the job scraper that hits public ATS APIs has one. An auditor reading README.md:326-334 would conclude the opposite. Setting `confirm_permission: false` in configs/leads/swiss-it.yaml changes nothing about whether the run proceeds.

**Fix.** Enforce it in src/leadgen/cli.py:main before any fetch, matching job_scraper/main.py:45-47, and add `--confirm-permission` to the lead parser. Or state plainly in the README that the gate applies to the job scraper only.

### ARCHITECTURE.md documents only the job scraper — the lead pipeline that generates the product's output is entirely absent

`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:64` · docs-mismatch

**What happens.** The high-level flow (lines 14-42) starts at `run.py` and the module map (lines 66-81) lists fourteen `src/job_scraper/` modules. `src/leadgen/` — 25 modules covering the seeds, the person cascade, email pattern inference, SMTP validation, scoring/quota, the checkpoint, net_guard and the CSV export — appears nowhere, nor does `run_leads.py`. Neither do the two newest modules, `src/job_scraper/transport.py` and `src/job_scraper/identity.py`. The map also lists `src/job_scraper/config.py` twice (lines 69 and 81) with different descriptions. Line 70 describes HttpClient as having a "per-host token bucket" — `HostThrottle` (http.py:~246) is an inflight cap plus a min-delay, with no burst capacity, so it is not a token bucket; README.md:140 and ARCHITECTURE.md:100 repeat the term.

**Failure.** An engineer onboarding to debug the lead famine reads ARCHITECTURE.md end to end, learns the job scraper's strategy stack in detail, and is never told that `run_leads.py -> src/leadgen/cli.py -> pipeline.process_companies -> person.cascade` exists — let alone that the cascade is where every company-site fetch happens and where the blocks land. Meanwhile src/leadgen/net_guard.py:5 asserts "the runbook says to run this in a container on a VPS", which no runbook says; ONBOARDING.md:33-37 tells employees to run it on their own Windows laptop.

**Fix.** Add a leadgen section with its own flow diagram (seed -> domain resolve -> cascade -> assemble -> score -> CSV) and a module table. Add transport.py and identity.py. Remove the duplicated config.py row. Replace "token bucket" with what HostThrottle is, or implement a token bucket.

### The runbook's --select-only recipe reads a checkpoint its own "Run it" command never writes

`docs/RUNBOOK-leads.md:261` · docs-mismatch

**What happens.** The Swiss "Run it" command at RUNBOOK-leads.md:222-225 passes no `--checkpoint`, so src/leadgen/cli.py:191 supplies the default `.cache/leadgen.sqlite`. The two recipes that are meant to operate on the results of that run both name a different file: line 261 (`--select-only --roles any --target 2000 --checkpoint .cache/swiss.sqlite`) and lines 292-294 (`--register ... --checkpoint .cache/swiss.sqlite`). Nothing in the document ever writes `.cache/swiss.sqlite`. src/leadgen/checkpoint.py:43-47 creates a missing path silently with an empty schema, so neither command errors.

**Failure.** An operator finishes the 35-page Swiss run, then runs the documented `--select-only` command to widen the cut to all roles. It opens an empty `.cache/swiss.sqlite`, selects from zero candidates, writes a header-only CSV, prints SHORTFALL 2000 and exits 2. The document claims immediately below (line 265) "On the run above it produced **2,000 rows**." The operator has no way to tell that the real 2,266 banked people are sitting in `.cache/leadgen.sqlite`.

**Fix.** Add `--checkpoint .cache/swiss.sqlite` to the "Run it" command at line 222, or drop it from lines 261 and 293. Consider making `--select-only` refuse to open a checkpoint path that does not already exist.

### RUNBOOK's jobs.ch page arithmetic is off by 2x and contradicts every other source in the repo

`docs/RUNBOOK-leads.md:210` · docs-mismatch

**What happens.** RUNBOOK-leads.md:207-210 states: "At the time of writing the filter reports ~1,440 matching jobs, 22 per page, so ~35 pages covers it." 1,440 / 22 = 65, not 35. Every other source in the repo says 65: scripts/daily.sh:39-40 ("~1,400 jobs = ~65 pages", `PAGES="${PAGES:-65}"`), scripts/daily.ps1:37-38 ("65 covers a 30-day window"), docs/ONBOARDING.md:264 ("65 covers a full 30-day window") and ONBOARDING.md:287 (30 days -> 1,396 postings -> 64 pages). The RUNBOOK then ships `--jobsch-pages 35` in its "Run it" command at line 224.

**Failure.** An operator following the RUNBOOK rather than the daily script runs `--jobsch-pages 35` and harvests roughly half the board — about 770 of ~1,400 postings — then reads the 'Measured yield' section (lines 245-253) which extrapolates from a 12-page run and concludes their yield is low for some other reason. Every downstream figure in that section is anchored to a page count the document got wrong.

**Fix.** Change 35 to 65 at lines 210 and 224, and at line 217 where `--jobsch-pages 40` is paired with a 7-day filter.

### RUNBOOK's troubleshooting and SMTP advice describe a seed and a flag that are not in its own commands

`docs/RUNBOOK-leads.md:132` · docs-mismatch

**What happens.** Two stale instructions. (1) The first troubleshooting entry (lines 132-138) says "**Every seed target returns 0 listings.** The seed is the Bundesagentur für Arbeit API," and directs the operator to add a path to `BASE_CANDIDATES` in src/job_scraper/adapters/arbeitsagentur.py. The Arbeitsagentur targets were removed from configs/leads/eu-it.yaml (see its lines 3-12) and the EU config now has `targets: []`, so the Arbeitsagentur adapter is never invoked by any documented lead command; docs/ONBOARDING.md:353-372 explains the removal at length. The advice sends the operator to edit an adapter that is not running. (2) Line 228 reads "Drop `--no-smtp` only where outbound port 25 is open" — but `--no-smtp` appears in neither of the two Swiss commands above it (lines 215-217 and 222-225), so SMTP probing is already on in both. The advice is inverted: it should say add `--no-smtp` unless port 25 is open, which is what scripts/daily.sh:55-58 and docs/ONBOARDING.md:65-66 say is the normal case.

**Failure.** An operator whose Europe run returns nothing reads the troubleshooting section, checks the Arbeitsagentur adapter's four BASE_CANDIDATES, finds nothing wrong, and never learns that the real reason is that the config has no targets at all and the command they ran passed no seed flags. Separately, a Swiss operator follows lines 222-225 verbatim on a network with port 25 blocked and pays an SMTP timeout per domain across the whole run.

**Fix.** Replace the first troubleshooting entry with one about a seedless command line (no --search-keywords / --jobsch-pages / --boards / --arbeitnow-pages). Invert line 228 to "Add `--no-smtp` unless outbound port 25 is open" and add it to the commands at :215 and :222.

### Re-scraping a posting wipes the operator's star, tags and notes

`extension/app/src/background/bulk.ts:170` · data-quality

**What happens.** Both save paths do a full-document db.jobs.put() with a job object built from emptyJob(), which initialises tags=[], notes="", starred=false. put() replaces the entire record, so when a fresh scrape produces the same fingerprint as an existing row, the user's annotations are silently destroyed. Nothing reads the existing row before writing.

**Failure.** Operator stars 40 promising leads on Monday. On Tuesday they re-run the same deep crawl to refresh salaries; every posting that still resolves to the same fingerprint comes back with starred=false, tags=[] and notes="". The triage work is gone with no warning and no undo.

**Fix.** In both save paths, read the existing row by id first and carry forward starred/tags/notes (and prefer mergeJobs for the content fields) before put(); or switch to db.jobs.update() for the schema fields so user columns are never touched.

### Cancelling a run (or losing the worker) leaves background tabs open forever and lets counters keep mutating a finished run

`extension/app/src/background/bulk.ts:222` · correctness

**What happens.** runHostScheduled breaks out of its loop on cancellation and waits only 5 seconds for in-flight work to settle; runCrawl then marks the run 'cancelled'/'done' and withKeepalive's finally clause drops activeHolds to 0 and clears the keepalive. But each in-flight visitInTab still holds an open tab with a timer of up to 45s (x up to 3 attempts). Those tabs are only closed by the timer or by a JOB_FOUND push — both of which live in the service worker that just lost its keepalive. There is also no chrome.tabs.onRemoved listener, so a tab the user closes manually is never settled and burns its full timeout.

**Failure.** Operator cancels a run with 4 tabs in flight. runCrawl returns, keepalive stops, and the worker is torn down ~30s later while the 45s timers are still pending. Four background tabs stay open in the user's window indefinitely. Repeat across runs and the browser accumulates dozens of orphan tabs.

**Fix.** Keep a Set of tabIds created by the crawl and close them all in runCrawl's finally block (and on chrome.runtime.onSuspend). Add a chrome.tabs.onRemoved listener that settles the matching PENDING/GENERAL_PENDING entry as a failure so a user-closed tab fails fast instead of timing out.

### CANCEL_RUN writes the run row outside the serialised counter chain and can roll back ok/done counts

`extension/app/src/background/main.ts:222` · correctness

**What happens.** makeCounters() exists precisely because concurrent get -> mutate -> put on the run row lost increments, and it serialises every counter write through one promise chain. The CANCEL_RUN handler bypasses that chain entirely: it does its own db.runs.get, mutates the snapshot, and puts it back. Any counter increment that lands between that get and put is overwritten by the stale snapshot.

**Failure.** A 500-URL run is cancelled at done=312. Four workers are mid-flight; two of them complete between the cancel handler's get and its put. The put writes back done=312 over done=314, and the Runs view shows fewer completed URLs than were actually saved to db.jobs — the counts no longer reconcile with the library.

**Fix.** Route cancellation through the same serialisation: keep a module-level map of runId -> counters and expose counters.patch({status:'cancelled'}), or wrap the read-modify-write in a Dexie readwrite transaction on db.runs so it is atomic against the counter writes.

### A job LIST page can score as a job DETAIL page, so runDetail() wins, one junk row is saved, and the list flow never runs

`extension/app/src/content/detector.ts:60` · correctness

**What happens.** detectJobPage returns isJob at confidence >= 0.6. A URL path hint contributes 0.3 and body text signals contribute up to 0.4, so a page with a /jobs path and four of the generic phrases ('apply now', 'requirements', 'what we offer', 'jetzt bewerben') reaches 0.62 with no JobPosting markup at all — and those phrases appear on listing pages because each card carries an Apply button. The ATS check adds another 0.3 for a regex match anywhere in the first 200 KB of outerHTML, so a footer link or tracking snippet mentioning smartrecruiters.com is enough on its own. main() then runs runDetail() first and returns early on success (inject.tsx:328-329), so runList() — and with it the whole card/pagination flow — never executes.

**Failure.** An operator opens https://careers.acme.de/jobs?loc=berlin, a 40-card listing. URL hint +0.3, four text signals +0.32 => 0.62 => isJob. extract() returns a job whose title is the page <title>, runDetail saves it and returns true, and the list banner with 'Deep-scrape all' / 'Scrape ALL pages' is never shown. The operator sees one meaningless row instead of 40 leads.

**Fix.** Gate runDetail on structured evidence (JSON-LD or microdata JobPosting) rather than the accumulated heuristic score, or run listCards() first and prefer runList() whenever it finds >= 2 cards. Restrict the ATS bonus to location.hostname instead of the whole HTML blob.

### expandAllContent() re-clicks the same elements up to five rounds with a 400ms sleep each, so a card-heavy list page stalls the tab for minutes

`extension/app/src/content/expandContent.ts:10` · perf

**What happens.** The function loops 5 times and, each round, re-queries findExpandButtons() and clicks EVERY match with a 400ms sleep between clicks plus a 600ms round delay. Clicked elements are never remembered, so any control whose label still reads 'show more' after the click (a toggle, or a no-op button) is clicked again every round. Callers await the result: main.ts awaits EXPAND_CONTENT before EXTRACT_NOW, and inject.tsx:222 awaits it inside the EXTRACT_NOW handler itself.

**Failure.** A list page where each of 50 cards carries a 'Mehr anzeigen' control: 5 rounds x 50 clicks x 400ms + 5 x 600ms = ~103 seconds. bulk.ts's visitInTab kills the tab at 45s, so the EXTRACT_NOW response never arrives and the URL is retried twice and then recorded as 'timeout' — a page that would have extracted fine.

**Fix.** Track clicked elements in a WeakSet and skip them on later rounds; cap total clicks (e.g. 25) and total wall time (e.g. 5s); reduce the per-click sleep to ~100ms. Also make EXPAND_CONTENT respond immediately and expand in the background rather than making the caller wait.

### dismissOverlays() auto-clicks cookie/GDPR consent buttons on every page the extension deems actionable

`extension/app/src/content/expandContent.ts:45` · correctness

**What happens.** The consent selector list targets any button inside an element whose class contains cookie/consent/gdpr plus #onetrust-accept-btn-handler, and clicks it when its text matches /accept|agree|ok|got it|close|dismiss/ OR is three characters or shorter. It is invoked from main() on every actionable page (inject.tsx:327), from EXTRACT_ACTIVE_TAB and EXTRACT_GENERAL in the background, and on every crawled page. It also clicks close buttons inside any fixed/absolute element whose class contains modal/overlay/popup, which is a broad class of buttons on unknown pages. For a product explicitly aimed at European sites and deployed on employees' own browser profiles, recording consent on the user's behalf is a real compliance exposure, not just a behavioural quirk.

**Failure.** An employee browses a German job board. The content script decides the page is actionable and clicks 'Alle akzeptieren' on the CMP, recording full tracking consent in that employee's own profile without them touching anything — and the click also happens inside any unrelated fixed-position dialog that happens to carry a 'modal' class.

**Fix.** Prefer reject/necessary-only buttons over accept ones, make consent clicking an explicit opt-in setting (default off) surfaced in Settings.tsx, and restrict the modal-close pass to elements that actually cover the viewport (bounding rect >= 50% of the window) instead of any fixed/absolute ancestor.

### In-site apply URLs ending in /apply or /application are discarded by the junk-path filter that is supposed to protect them

`extension/app/src/content/extractor.ts:74` · correctness

**What happens.** bestApplyUrl accepts an in-site candidate when APPLY_PATH_RX (/\/(apply|application|bewerb|postuler|candidat|submission)/) matches AND !isJunkUrl(abs). But NON_JOB_PATH_RX — used by isJunkUrl — contains `applications?`, so any URL whose path ends in /application or /applications is classified as junk and rejected. The two regexes directly contradict each other for a large class of ATS apply links.

**Failure.** A Personio/Recruitee-style posting whose only apply link is https://jobs.acme.de/stelle/12345/application. matchPath is true, isJunkUrl is also true, so inSite stays empty and apply_url falls back to job_url — the detail page rather than the application form. The lead loses its actionable field.

**Fix.** Narrow NON_JOB_PATH_RX to /my-?applications?|\/account\/applications?/ so it only catches the applicant's own dashboard, or skip the isJunkUrl check on candidates that already matched APPLY_PATH_RX. Delete the two dead alternatives that test for '?' inside a pathname.

### The description fallback selector picks the first matching element in document order, which is often a search form or nav block

`extension/app/src/content/extractor.ts:142` · data-quality

**What happens.** When the structured description is short, the code calls document.querySelector() with an 8-way comma list including [id*='job'] and [class*='description']. querySelector returns the first element in DOCUMENT ORDER matching ANY selector, not the first selector's match — so a header-level `<div id="job-search-form">` or a meta-description wrapper near the top of the page wins over `<main>`.

**Failure.** A German careers page renders `<div id="jobsuche-filter">` above `<main>`. The description column is filled with the filter widget's label text ('Ort, Umkreis, Berufsfeld, Anstellungsart, Suchen') instead of the posting body, and the row still passes the length > 50 gate so nothing flags it.

**Fix.** Try each selector in priority order in a loop (main, [role='main'], article, then the class heuristics) and pick the candidate with the most text rather than the first in document order; require a minimum length like 200 chars before accepting a heuristic container.

### The Norwegian contact miner runs on every page and its phone pattern fires on any dd dd dd dd digit run

`extension/app/src/content/extractor.ts:101` · data-quality

**What happens.** mineNorwegianContacts is called unconditionally on every extraction ('always run'). Its name/title mining is safely gated on the Norwegian-only token 'Stillingstittel', but the phone regex is not gated at all — it matches any `\d{2} \d{2} \d{2} \d{2}` anywhere in the first 30,000 chars of body text on any site in any language. Note also the asymmetry: recruiter_name and recruiter_title overwrite existing values unconditionally, while only recruiter_phone is guarded by a !job.recruiter_phone check.

**Failure.** A German posting whose sidebar lists an IBAN-style or reference run such as '12 34 56 78' has recruiter_phone set to '12345678'. It looks like a valid 8-digit number, passes every downstream check, and is dialled by the sales team.

**Fix.** Gate the whole miner on a Norwegian signal (documentElement.lang starting with 'no'/'nb'/'nn', a .no host, or the literal 'Stillingstittel'/'Kontaktperson' token) before running the phone regex, and add the same `&& !job.recruiter_name` guard to the name and title assignments that the phone assignment already has.

### waitForJobContent() polls with requestAnimationFrame, which is suspended in the background tabs the crawler creates

`extension/app/src/content/inject.tsx:314` · correctness

**What happens.** main() awaits waitForJobContent(), whose poll loop reschedules itself with requestAnimationFrame. Chrome does not run rAF callbacks in hidden/background tabs. bulk.ts opens every crawl target with chrome.tabs.create({url, active:false}), so those tabs are hidden and the loop stalls after its first synchronous pass — main() never proceeds, runDetail() never runs, and the JOB_FOUND self-push (which bulk.ts's design comment relies on as the fast path) never fires for crawled pages. The crawl only still works because of the single 8s poke, and even the 8s timeout is not retried.

**Failure.** A slow EU careers page finishes rendering at t=12s. The single poke fires at t=8s, the content script has nothing yet and returns no job, and nothing pokes again. The visit burns the full 45s, is retried twice with 2s and 4s backoff, and is finally recorded as reason='no-content' — ~141s spent to fail a page that was actually fine.

**Fix.** Replace requestAnimationFrame with setTimeout in waitForJobContent so it advances in hidden tabs, and change the single poke in visitInTab into a poll (e.g. every 3s until timeoutMs) so a page that becomes ready after the first poke is captured instead of timing out.

### Paginator.clickAndWait() returns true after an 8s timeout with no DOM change, so a dead Next button burns the whole page budget

`extension/app/src/content/paginator.ts:192` · correctness

**What happens.** clickAndWait watches document.querySelectorAll('a[href]').length for 8 seconds; if nothing changed it still returns true with the comment 'timeout but still try'. collectAll therefore treats a click that did nothing as a successful page advance and loops again, re-extracting the same cards (all filtered out by the `seen` set) until maxPages is exhausted.

**Failure.** A disabled-but-visible Next control on the last results page. 100 iterations x (8s wait + 1.8s delay) = ~16 minutes of no-op looping on the user's tab before collectAll finishes with the page-1 cards.

**Fix.** Return false on the timeout path, and have collectAll treat two consecutive pages that yield zero new cards as end-of-list regardless of what advance() claims.

### Image filenames are harvested as e-mail addresses because the junk-email filter is narrower than Python's

`extension/app/src/content/strategies/heuristics.ts:103` · data-quality

**What happens.** EMAIL_RX matches any `word@word.tld`, which includes retina asset names (icon@2x.png), Sentry DSN fragments and similar. Python guards with JUNK_EMAIL_RX = /noreply|no-reply|donotreply|example\.com|@sentry|\.png$|\.jpg$/i (extract.py:1103). The extension only filters /noreply|no-reply|example\.com/, so the image and sentry cases pass straight into application_email.

**Failure.** A careers page whose body text includes an inline srcset string yields application_email="icon@2x.png". That row goes into the CSV the operator feeds to an outreach tool, which bounces it and damages the sending domain's reputation.

**Fix.** Copy JUNK_EMAIL_RX from extract.py into heuristics.ts (and into generalExtract.ts:121, which has the same narrow filter), and add Python's `personal` alternative to the recruiter-email pattern while you are there.

### Any job description mentioning "distributed" is labelled remote

`extension/app/src/content/strategies/heuristics.ts:40` · data-quality

**What happens.** REMOTE_RX includes the bare word `distributed`, which appears in the phrase 'distributed systems' in a very large share of engineering job descriptions. remote_type is set to 'remote' before HYBRID_RX/ONSITE_RX are even considered, so an explicitly on-site role is mislabelled. (The same alternative exists in src/job_scraper/extract.py:1118, so this is a shared defect rather than a divergence — it needs fixing on both sides.)

**Failure.** A Berlin on-site backend role whose requirements say 'experience building distributed systems' exports remote_type='remote'. The operator's 'Remote only' filter in Library.tsx:33 surfaces it as a remote lead.

**Fix.** Remove the bare `distributed` alternative (require 'distributed team' / 'fully distributed'), and evaluate ONSITE_RX first so an explicit on-site statement wins over an incidental keyword. Fix src/job_scraper/extract.py:1117 identically.

### Seniority is decided by dictionary order, not by what the posting is actually hiring for

`extension/app/src/content/strategies/heuristics.ts:75` · data-quality

**What happens.** The SENIORITY list is scanned in array order and the first regex that matches anywhere in the full page/description text wins. 'senior' is checked before 'junior', and 'staff' — an everyday English noun — is checked third. The match is not anchored to the title, so boilerplate anywhere on the page decides the label.

**Failure.** A graduate programme page that says 'you will be mentored by senior engineers' is exported as seniority='senior'. Filtering the library for junior roles returns nothing.

**Fix.** Run the seniority match against job.title first and fall back to the description only when the title is silent; order the checks by specificity (intern, junior, mid, senior, staff, principal, lead, head, director) and drop the bare /\bstaff\b/ in favour of /\bstaff\s+(engineer|software|data|product)/.

### The tech-stack scan runs one full regex pass per dictionary term over 200 KB of text, on every page load of every site

`extension/app/src/content/strategies/heuristics.ts:69` · perf

**What happens.** TECH_DICT.filter() constructs a fresh RegExp per term and tests it against the whole lowercased page text (up to 200,000 chars). With ~110 terms that is ~22 MB of scanning plus 110 RegExp compilations, synchronously on the page's main thread. Python fixed exactly this: extract.py builds a single longest-first alternation with the comment "Scanning 200 KB of text once per term (330 terms) cost ~66 MB of regex work per page; one pass costs ~200 KB." The extension never received that fix, and because the content script matches <all_urls> and main() calls extract() via looksActionable(), it runs on ordinary browsing too.

**Failure.** Employee opens a long documentation page that trips looksActionable(). extract() runs, the tech scan blocks the main thread for a noticeable pause, and the same cost is paid again on every SPA navigation (onNavigated re-runs main()). Across a crawl of hundreds of hidden tabs this is the dominant CPU cost.

**Fix.** Precompile one longest-first alternation RegExp at module load (mirroring TECH_RX), run a single matchAll pass, and map hits back through a lowercase lookup table — the same structure Python already uses.

### Recruiter detection has no German or French title vocabulary, so EU-language postings yield no recruiter contact

`extension/app/src/content/strategies/recruiter.ts:4` · coverage

**What happens.** RECRUITER_TITLE_RX in the DOM recruiter strategy covers only English terms. src/job_scraper/extract.py:400-404 (and the extension's own jsonld.ts:13 RECRUITER_TITLE_HINTS) additionally cover Personalleit, Personalverantwort, HR-Team, Personalabteilung, Leiter HR, HR Business Partner, HR Generalist, Talent Lead and 'Chef du/de personnel'. The DOM path — the one that runs when the recruiter block is in the page rather than inside the JSON-LD description HTML — was never updated to match. The recruiter-email fallback is likewise missing Python's `personal` alternative.

**Failure.** A German SME careers page shows a contact card reading 'Ihre Ansprechpartnerin: Sabine Keller, Personalleiterin, s.keller@firma.de'. RECRUITER_TITLE_RX does not match 'Personalleiterin', the loop `continue`s, and recruiter_name/title/email all come out empty — the single most valuable field for a lead-gen product, on the exact market the operator is targeting.

**Fix.** Replace RECRUITER_TITLE_RX with the same pattern already present at jsonld.ts:13, and add `personal` to the recruiter-email pattern. Better: hoist one shared constant so the DOM strategy and the JSON-LD strategy cannot drift again.

### The extension's mergeJobs has no first-wins list, breaking the documented CLI/extension lock-step

`extension/app/src/lib/schema.ts:61` · docs-mismatch

**What happens.** models.py's docstring states the schema and fingerprint are kept in lock-step with the extension and that CSVs can be concatenated. JOB_FIELDS order and fingerprint() do match. But mergeJobs applies `if (!cur || str.length > cur.length)` to EVERY field, with no equivalent of _FIRST_WINS_FIELDS — so dates, URLs, salary figures and identifiers take the longer string in the extension and the first value in the CLI. Since merge feeds title/company/location, the two halves can derive different fingerprints for the same posting.

**Failure.** The extension scrapes a posting where one source gives posted_date '2026-05-01' and another gives 'Veröffentlicht am 1. Mai 2026'. The extension stores the longer prose string; the CLI stores the ISO date. The same posting exports two different posted_date values and, if title/location also diverge, two different ids — the exact split the comment warns about.

**Fix.** Port _FIRST_WINS_FIELDS into schema.ts and apply the same rule in mergeJobs, or move both to a shared generated constant.

### Exporting with a filter that matches nothing silently exports the entire library instead

`extension/app/src/views/Library.tsx:71` · correctness

**What happens.** All three export functions use `filtered.length ? filtered : allJobs`. The ternary is meant to mean 'no filter active', but it actually fires whenever the filter matched ZERO rows — the two states are indistinguishable at that point. So a narrowing filter that returns nothing silently widens the export to everything.

**Failure.** Operator filters the library to company='Zalando', which matches 0 rows because company was polluted with the board name. They click Export CSV expecting an empty or tiny file and instead hand the client a CSV containing all 40,000 harvested rows including unrelated clients' leads.

**Fix.** Track whether any filter is active (q/remoteOnly/sourceFilter/starredOnly) and export `filtered` whenever one is, even when it is empty; only fall back to allJobs when no filter is set. Show the row count in the button label so the operator can see what is about to be written.

### Manifest requests permissions the code never uses and exposes the whole bundle to every website

`extension/manifest.json:6` · security

**What happens.** The manifest declares `scripting` and `activeTab`, and declares web_accessible_resources for `dist/*` and `public/*` matched to <all_urls>. Grepping app/src shows chrome.scripting is never called, chrome.runtime.getURL is never called, and no page resource is referenced from a web page — so nothing in the extension needs any of the three. Declaring dist/* as web-accessible means any website can fetch chrome-extension://<id>/dist/background.js and dist/sidepanel.html, which both (a) makes the extension trivially fingerprintable by any page (a standard anti-bot detection signal, directly relevant to this product's purpose) and (b) hands any site the full bundled source. `scripting` additionally widens the install-time warning and the store review surface for no benefit.

**Failure.** A job board's anti-bot script probes chrome-extension://<known-id>/dist/sidepanel.html from the page. The fetch succeeds (no use_dynamic_url is set), the site fingerprints the scraper extension and serves the operator a bot wall on every subsequent visit — which is exactly the failure mode the product is trying to escape.

**Fix.** Delete the web_accessible_resources block entirely (nothing references it); if a resource is ever needed, list only that file and set "use_dynamic_url": true. Drop `scripting` and `activeTab` from permissions. Consider narrowing host_permissions from <all_urls> to an optional_host_permissions flow so the operator grants per-site access.

### General JSON-LD mapping writes a joined sameAs list into `website`, a boolean into `reservation_url`, and a dict repr into `menu_url`

`src/general_scraper/extract.py:229` · data-quality

**What happens.** Three schema.org fields are read without regard for their declared types. `item.get("url") or item.get("sameAs")` falls back to `sameAs`, which is a *list of social profile URLs*, and `_str` comma-joins a list. `acceptsReservations` is a Boolean (or a URL) in schema.org, and `_first_url` on a bool returns the bool, which `_str` renders as `"True"`. `hasMenu` is commonly a `Menu` object, and `_str` renders a dict with Python's repr. All three land in the exported CSV columns of the same name.

**Failure.** A restaurant emits `{"sameAs": ["https://facebook.com/x", "https://instagram.com/x"], "acceptsReservations": true, "hasMenu": {"@type": "Menu", "url": "https://x/menu"}}` and no top-level `url`. The exported row has `website = "https://facebook.com/x, https://instagram.com/x"` (not a website, and unusable for domain matching or email-pattern inference), `reservation_url = "True"`, and `menu_url = "{'@type': 'Menu', 'url': 'https://x/menu'}"`.

**Fix.** Drop the `sameAs` fallback for `website` (it already feeds `social_links`) and validate the result parses as an http(s) URL. For `acceptsReservations`, only keep it when it is a string URL or a `Reservation`/`Action` object's `url`; for `hasMenu`, unwrap `dict → .get('url')` (and list → first element) before stringifying. Add a shared `_url_str(v)` helper that returns "" for anything that is not a URL.

### General heuristics accept any 7+ digit run as a phone number and the first email on the page as the business email

`src/general_scraper/extract.py:339` · data-quality

**What happens.** `PHONE_RX` is three loosely-separated digit groups with everything optional in front; the only sanity check is `len(re.sub(r"\D","",match)) >= 7`. Any 7-digit-or-longer number in the first 50,000 characters of body text qualifies — VAT/company-register numbers, order ids, product SKUs, postal+street combinations, price tables. `_apply_heuristics` takes the *first* match in document order, which on most sites is in the header or nav, not the business block. The email heuristic has the same first-match-wins problem, filtered only against `noreply|no-reply|example.com`.

**Failure.** A German business page opens with "USt-IdNr. DE 123456789" in the header. `rec.phone` becomes `"123456789"`. The record ships with a VAT number in the phone column; an outbound dialer burns a call slot on it, and any downstream dedupe keyed on phone (`GeneralRecord.fingerprint` includes phone) merges unrelated businesses that happen to share a boilerplate number.

**Fix.** Prefer explicit sources first (`tel:` hrefs, `itemprop=telephone`, `mailto:` hrefs) and only fall back to regex within an address/contact container. Require a leading `+`/`0`/parenthesised area code, validate per-country length, and reject matches adjacent to VAT/tax/register keywords. For email, prefer `mailto:` links and same-domain addresses and skip obvious role addresses from cookie/privacy boilerplate.

### JSON-LD ItemList extraction misses the list-valued `@type` form and ListItem entries without an `item`

`src/general_scraper/extract.py:104` · coverage

**What happens.** `str(block.get("@type", "")).lower() == "itemlist"` only matches a plain string. Sites frequently publish `"@type": ["ItemList", "CollectionPage"]`, for which `str([...]).lower()` is `"['itemlist', 'collectionpage']"` and the comparison fails — even though the neighbouring `_is_business` helper already handles the list form correctly. Inside the list, only `el["item"]` is considered, so the very common `{"@type":"ListItem","position":1,"url":"https://..."}` shape (URL only, no nested item) yields nothing.

**Failure.** A directory listing page publishes a correct `ItemList` with `"@type": ["ItemList","SearchResultsPage"]`. The structured path silently yields zero records and control falls through to `_heuristic_card_extract`, which guesses from CSS class names and picks the first anchor in each card — so the run produces lower-quality rows (often the wrong `source_url`) from a page that had machine-readable data all along.

**Fix.** Normalise `@type` through the same list-aware helper `_is_business` uses. Accept `ListItem` entries with only a `url` (emit a record carrying just `source_url` so deep-scrape can fill it), and recurse into nested `ItemList`s.

### General scraper: one bad fetch ends a target's pagination, and one bad CSS selector aborts the entire run

`src/general_scraper/main.py:169` · correctness

**What happens.** Two isolation gaps in the same loop. (1) `_collect_cards` does `if fetched is None: break` — a single transient failure (403, timeout, one proxy hiccup) ends pagination for that target, and general mode has no retry layer at all (unlike job mode's `max_retries`). (2) `extract_listing_cards` deliberately *raises* `ValueError` for an invalid `card` selector, and neither `_collect_cards` nor the `for target in config.targets` loop catches it; the enclosing `try` has only a `finally`. Records are written after the loop, so the whole run is lost. This is inconsistent with `_pick`, which swallows selector errors by design.

**Failure.** Target 1 of 5 has a typo in its `card` selector. Targets 2-5 would have worked, but the ValueError from target 1 propagates out of `main()`; `output/general.csv` is never written and the operator gets a traceback instead of four targets' worth of records.

**Fix.** Wrap each target's body in `try/except Exception: logging.warning(...); continue`. In `_collect_cards`, retry a failed page once or twice before breaking, and log which page failed. Make `extract_listing_cards` validate the selector once up front (at config load, via `soupsieve.compile`) rather than raising mid-run.

### General scraper deep-scrape is single-threaded, retry-less and unguarded

`src/general_scraper/main.py:136` · perf

**What happens.** `_deep_scrape` is a plain `for card in cards` loop with one `_fetch` each. `HttpClient._global_pace` enforces `delay_seconds` (default 1.0) between requests process-wide, so throughput is hard-capped at ~1 detail page per second regardless of machine or proxy count. There is no retry on a failed fetch (just `continue`) and no try/except around `extract_record_from_page`, so a parser exception on one business page kills the run before anything is exported. Job mode solved all three with `ThreadPoolExecutor` + `HostThrottle` + retries in `deep_scrape.py`; general mode never got the same treatment.

**Failure.** A 3,000-business directory run: deep-scrape alone takes ≥50 minutes of pure pacing (and far longer with Playwright's ~12 s per page), during which a single malformed page raising out of `extract_record_from_page` discards all 3,000 records because `_write_csv` runs only after the target loop.

**Fix.** Reuse `deep_scrape.HostThrottle` + a `ThreadPoolExecutor` sized from config, add the same bounded retry/backoff, and wrap each card's work in try/except so one page cannot end the run.

### General scraper's CSV write is non-atomic and truncates the previous good output before it has any rows

`src/general_scraper/main.py:223` · correctness

**What happens.** `_write_csv` opens the destination with mode `"w"`, which truncates immediately, then streams rows. Job mode's `export_csv` was explicitly hardened against this (temp file in the destination directory + `os.replace`), but the general path never got the fix. A crash, a full disk, or simply a run that collected zero records replaces yesterday's usable file with a truncated or header-only one.

**Failure.** An overnight run hits a 403 wall on page 1 of every target and collects nothing. `_write_csv` still runs (it is not gated on `records`), truncating the previous night's 4,000-row `output/general.csv` down to a bare header. The prior data is unrecoverable.

**Fix.** Reuse `job_scraper.export.csv_exporter.export_csv`'s temp-file-plus-`os.replace` pattern, and skip the write (with a warning) when `records` is empty.

### finn.no discards the target URL's path, and /job/fulltime/ no longer restricts to full-time

`src/job_scraper/adapters/finn.py:29` · correctness

**What happens.** `FinnNoAdapter` rebuilds every request as `SEARCH_BASE + "?" + urlencode(params)`, keeping only the query string from the configured URL and throwing the path away. Meanwhile finn.no has unified its job verticals: `/job/fulltime/search.html` now 301s to a bare `/job/search` with no filter attached, whereas `/job/parttime/search.html` 301s to `/job/search?extent=3942` (the filter is preserved as a query parameter for that vertical but not for full-time). The adapter's hardcoded base therefore no longer means what its name and docstring say.

**Failure.** Two failures. (a) A config pointing at `https://www.finn.no/job/parttime/search.html?...` or `/job/management/search.html?...` is silently rewritten to the full-time base — the path-encoded filter is dropped and the user gets a different search than the one they configured. (b) Every existing finn target that says "fulltime" now also harvests part-time, temporary and management ads; ~18% of the first page of a 'fulltime' result is part-time.

**Fix.** Preserve the path from `target.url` (fall back to SEARCH_BASE only when the URL has no path), point SEARCH_BASE at `https://www.finn.no/job/search`, and if full-time really is wanted, encode it as the query parameter finn now uses rather than relying on a path that no longer filters.

### finn.no rows say country='NO' while the other three Nordic adapters say 'Norway' in the same CSV

`src/job_scraper/adapters/finn.py:71` · data-quality

**What happens.** nav.py:41, karrierestart.py:39 and jobbsafari.py:37 all seed `country="Norway"` on the stub. finn.py does not, so finn rows inherit whatever JSON-LD says — the ISO code `NO` from `addressCountry`. `country` is not in `_FIRST_WINS_FIELDS`, and 'NO' is shorter than 'Norway', so nothing later corrects it.

**Failure.** `normalize.match_location` uses word-boundary matching over `location_blob`. A config with `locations.include: [norway, norge]` and `require_match: true` drops every finn.no row whose city is not independently listed — the country filter matches 'Norway' on nav rows and misses 'NO' on finn rows. configs/regions/norway-big.yaml and norway-top5.yaml are exactly this shape.

**Fix.** Seed `country="Norway"` in the finn adapter for parity with the other three, and normalise ISO-3166 alpha-2 country codes to full names centrally in `extract._apply_jsonld` so the CSV never mixes the two representations.

### generic.py Playwright escalation ignores a per-target opt-out and double-crawls when the target opts in

`src/job_scraper/adapters/generic.py:76` · correctness

**What happens.** `for use_pw in (False if target_pw is None else target_pw, True)` builds (False, True) when target.use_playwright is False - so an explicit per-target 'do not use a browser here' still gets a browser on the second iteration, because the break at line 100 is gated on run_config.use_playwright, not on the target override. When target.use_playwright is True the tuple is (True, True), so an unproductive Playwright crawl is repeated verbatim.

**Failure.** A site the operator marked use_playwright: false (because the browser gets it CAPTCHA'd or it is known static) is still driven with Chromium; and a JS-heavy site marked use_playwright: true that legitimately has no jobs pays for two full headless crawls.

**Fix.** Compute the passes explicitly: effective = target.use_playwright if not None else run_config.use_playwright; run the plain pass, then escalate only if effective is not False and the plain pass produced nothing, and never repeat the same mode twice.

### Sitemap-derived URLs are fetched with no domain restriction, so a robots.txt Sitemap: line can redirect the crawl to any host

`src/job_scraper/adapters/generic.py:52` · security

**What happens.** discovery.fetch_sitemap_urls (discovery.py:107-114) appends every `Sitemap:` URL found in robots.txt to the fetch queue, and every <loc> in every sitemap it reaches is returned regardless of host. generic.py:52 then fetches job_urls[:max_pages] directly with http.get. run_config.allow_domains is only consulted in step 3 (the Crawler), never here.

**Failure.** A site whose robots.txt lists a sitemap on a CDN or partner host (common) sends the crawler off-domain; a hostile or compromised site can point the crawler at an arbitrary third party and have the run's IP/proxy pool fetch whatever it lists under a /jobs/ path.

**Fix.** Filter sitemap URLs to the target's allow_domains (or the target host plus its registrable suffix) before fetching, and apply the same check to the Sitemap: directives themselves.

### Greenhouse slug resolution wastes 5-6 requests per board and can attribute another company's board to the requested slug

`src/job_scraper/adapters/greenhouse.py:63` · correctness

**What happens.** _resolve first fetches .../jobs (a different URL from the .../jobs?content=true the caller then fetches, so it is never a cache hit), then guesses slug.replace('-',''), slug+'inc', slug+'ag', slug+'se', slug+'gmbh'. A hit on a guess replaces the slug and becomes `company` on every listing, so a board belonging to a different legal entity is filed under the requested company with no record of the substitution.

**Failure.** 837 greenhouse slugs in configs/leads/boards.txt x ~5MB per successful board + 5 probe calls per miss. A miss on 'acme' that happens to hit an unrelated 'acmeag' board files a stranger's jobs (and, downstream, a stranger's staff) under Acme.

**Fix.** Probe with the same URL the fetch will use so the cache absorbs it, and drop the legal-suffix guessing (or keep it only when the guessed board's company_name fuzzy-matches the requested name, and log the substitution).

### Greenhouse sets company to the board slug although every job carries company_name

`src/job_scraper/adapters/greenhouse.py:43` · data-quality

**What happens.** `company=slug` uses the URL token. The live API returns company_name on every job object. The slug is a lowercase, punctuation-stripped identifier, so company matching, dedupe against other sources, and domain resolution downstream all work from a mangled name.

**Failure.** guess_domains and the person cascade start from 'getyourguide' instead of 'GetYourGuide', and the same employer appearing via a job-search source as 'GetYourGuide GmbH' never dedupes against the Greenhouse rows.

**Fix.** company = item.get('company_name') or slug, and keep the slug in a separate source_slug field.

### jobs.ch JSON API fallback is robots-disallowed and therefore dead under the default config

`src/job_scraper/adapters/jobsch.py:28` · silent-failure

**What happens.** `API_BASE = "https://www.jobs.ch/api/v1/public/search"` sits under `/api/`, which jobs.ch's robots.txt disallows. `RunConfig.obey_robots` defaults to True (config.py:39), so any run that does not explicitly opt out — `--urls https://www.jobs.ch/...`, a fresh config, any caller other than configs/sites/jobsch.yaml — gets `None` from every API request at the robots gate (a DEBUG-level line) and reports "empty payload at page 1".

**Failure.** The adapter docstring calls the API path the "fallback when the HTML path can't be parsed". The moment the HTML path breaks — a markup change that retires DETAIL_RX — the fallback silently yields nothing too, because nobody notices the robots gate. The adapter returns [] for both paths and logs only "jobs.ch API: empty payload at page 1".

**Fix.** Either drop the API path from the adapter and document the HTML path as the only supported route, or have `_fetch_via_api` check `http.robots_allows(API_BASE)` up front and log a clear WARNING naming robots as the reason so it is never mistaken for an outage.

### jobs.ch API hard-caps at page 100 / rows 20 while advertising num_pages 2128; the 422 is reported as an empty payload

`src/job_scraper/adapters/jobsch.py:104` · correctness

**What happens.** `_fetch_via_api` defaults to `max_pages = run_config.max_pages or 200` and relies on `payload.get("num_pages")` to terminate. Live, the server reports `num_pages: 2128` but rejects `page > 100` and any `rows != 20` with HTTP 422. `http.get` turns 422 into `None`, `get_json` returns `None`, and the adapter logs `"jobs.ch API: empty payload at page %d"` — which reads as "the corpus ran out" rather than "the server refused". The `num_pages` check at jobsch.py:125-127 is therefore unreachable.

**Failure.** An operator sets `run.max_pages: 200` expecting 4,000 Swiss postings via the API path. The run stops at 2,000 with the message "empty payload at page 101" and the operator concludes the corpus is exhausted, when 42,541 exist. Additionally, if any caller passes a `rows=` param in the target URL, `_extract_query_params` forwards it and the adapter appends a second `rows=20` (jobsch.py:108) — a duplicate parameter whose resolution is server-defined and which 422s outright if the server takes the first value.

**Fix.** Clamp `max_pages` to 100 and `rows` to exactly 20 in the adapter, strip any inbound `page`/`rows` from the user URL before appending your own, and log "jobs.ch API: server refused page %d (HTTP 422) — hard cap is 100 pages x 20 rows = 2000 results" instead of "empty payload".

### Lever reads allLocations at the top level but the API only ever puts it under categories

`src/job_scraper/adapters/lever.py:47` · data-quality

**What happens.** `all_locations = item.get('allLocations') or []` is always empty, so the branch at line 48-49 that would join every location is dead and `location` stays whatever single value categories.location holds. Multi-location postings lose all but one location.

**Failure.** A Lever posting advertised in Berlin, Amsterdam and Stockholm is filed with only its primary location, so a geography-filtered EU run misses it for two of three countries.

**Fix.** Read categories.allLocations (falling back to categories.location), and add a fixture test built from a captured live response so a shape change is caught.

### Recruitee builds requests to unrelated third-party domains (careers.{slug}.com) and mis-parses uppercase hosts

`src/job_scraper/adapters/recruitee.py:74` · security

**What happens.** _fetch_offers falls back to `https://careers.{c}.com/api/offers` for each of three slug candidates. That host is synthesised from a slug and belongs to whoever owns {slug}.com, not to the Recruitee tenant. Separately, _extract_slug (line 84) uses parsed.netloc without lowercasing, so an uppercase host fails the .recruitee.com endswith test and the whole hostname becomes the slug.

**Failure.** Scraping 'apple' as a Recruitee slug issues a request to careers.apple.com/api/offers; at scale the run probes hundreds of unrelated corporate domains, and any JSON they happen to return with an 'offers' list is ingested as that company's jobs.

**Fix.** Lowercase the host before matching, and remove the careers.{slug}.com guess (or restrict it to a domain explicitly supplied on the target config).

### SmartRecruiters slug resolution makes 24-32 live API calls per unmatched company and can silently resolve to a different company's board

`src/job_scraper/adapters/smartrecruiters.py:48` · correctness

**What happens.** _resolve_slug treats 'totalFound == 0' as 'slug does not exist' (line 69-74), but SmartRecruiters returns 200 with totalFound:0 for BOTH a non-existent company and a real company with no open roles. It then brute-forces 4 case variants x 8 suffixes ('', Group, SE, AG, GmbH, Inc, Holding, International), returning the first that has postings - which may be a different legal entity than the one requested. The fabricated slug then becomes the company identity everywhere downstream.

**Failure.** A seed of 200 companies of which 150 are not on SmartRecruiters issues ~4,500 wasted API calls (~20 min) against a host whose robots.txt already forbids you, and any company whose real board is empty today gets silently re-pointed at 'NameGroup' or 'NameHolding' if such a board exists.

**Fix.** Distinguish 404/unknown from totalFound:0 (query /v1/companies/{id} which does 404), drop the suffix brute force or move it behind an explicit --guess-slugs flag, and record the resolved slug on the listing so a wrong resolution is visible in the output.

### Offset pagination has no mid-run failure handling: one 429 or 5xx silently truncates a board

`src/job_scraper/adapters/smartrecruiters.py:33` · silent-failure

**What happens.** The SmartRecruiters loop breaks on `payload is None` (line 33-34) and the Workday loop breaks on `data is None` (workday.py:39-40). http.get/post_json return None for 429, 5xx after retries, WAF bodies and network errors alike, so a transient failure on page 27 of 49 is indistinguishable from the end of the board. Neither adapter compares what it collected against totalFound/total.

**Failure.** A 429 partway through a large board yields, say, 2,600 of 4,801 jobs and logs 'smartrecruiters BoschGroup: 2600 jobs' as if that were the whole board; the following week's diff then reports 2,200 jobs as newly created.

**Fix.** Retry the page, and if it still fails raise or mark the result partial; assert collected >= totalFound (allowing drift) and log a warning when short; dedupe on posting id to absorb the shifting-offset overlap.

### Workday silently returns nothing for URLs without a locale segment or with a port, and the dispatcher accepts hosts the parser rejects

`src/job_scraper/adapters/workday.py:83` · silent-failure

**What happens.** _extract_parts requires host to match ^([a-z0-9-]+)\.(wd\d+)\.myworkdayjobs\.com$ and path to have >=2 segments. adapters/__init__.py:70 routes on the far broader `'myworkdayjobs.com' in host`. Any mismatch returns None at line 29-30 with no log line at all, so the target looks like an empty board.

**Failure.** An operator pastes the career-site URL exactly as their browser shows it (no /en-US/ prefix on many tenants) and gets 0 jobs with no indication the URL was never parsed.

**Fix.** Log a warning naming the URL when _extract_parts fails; accept a missing locale by treating path[0] as the site when there is only one segment; strip the port before matching; and distinguish a 4xx from the CXS endpoint ('wrong site id') from an empty board in the log.

### Workday posted_date is a human-readable relative string, not a date

`src/job_scraper/adapters/workday.py:64` · data-quality

**What happens.** `posted_date=item.get('postedOn') or ''` stores the CXS field verbatim, which is prose like 'Posted Today' / 'Posted 30+ Days Ago'. Every other adapter stores an ISO timestamp in the same field, so any sort, range filter or 'newer than' comparison over mixed sources is wrong for Workday rows.

**Failure.** A 'posted in the last 14 days' filter either drops all Workday rows (string does not parse) or keeps all of them (no parse attempted), and CSV consumers sorting by posted_date get 'Posted Today' ordered alphabetically among ISO timestamps.

**Fix.** Parse the relative phrase into a date at ingest (or read startDate/postedOn from the detail endpoint's jobPostingInfo, which carries a real date), and keep the raw string in a separate field.

### `_expand_content` never expands anything on a page with more than one "Read more" button — strict-mode violation

`src/job_scraper/crawl.py:195` · correctness

**What happens.** `page.get_by_role("button", name=label)` is a Playwright locator in strict mode: if it resolves to more than one element, `.click()` raises `strict mode violation` instead of clicking the first. The exception is swallowed by `with suppress(Exception)`, so the description stays collapsed and nothing is logged. Job pages routinely have several collapsible sections sharing one label, which is precisely when expansion matters.

**Failure.** A Workday or karrierestart posting renders "Read more" on both the job description and the company blurb. Every click attempt raises, the description stays truncated at the fold, and the exported `description` is the teaser instead of the full text — which also starves the keyword filter (`text_blob` includes `description`), so the posting is dropped by `keywords.include`.

**Fix.** Use `loc = page.get_by_role("button", name=label); for i in range(min(loc.count(), 5)): loc.nth(i).click(timeout=800)`, or `.first.click()`, and skip the label entirely when `count() == 0` so no timeout is paid.

### Cookie-banner dismissal only searches the main frame and runs before the CMP has rendered

`src/job_scraper/crawl.py:164` · anti-bot

**What happens.** `_dismiss_overlays` uses `page.query_selector(sel)`, which searches the main frame only. The dominant European consent platforms (Sourcepoint, Didomi, TrustArc, and Cookiebot/OneTrust in their iframe modes) render their dialog inside a cross-origin iframe, so none of the 16 selectors can ever match. It is also called immediately after `page.goto(..., wait_until='domcontentloaded')`, *before* `_wait_for_job_content` — CMP scripts load asynchronously, so on most sites the banner has not been injected yet when the one and only dismissal attempt runs. The label list also covers only English, German ("Akzeptieren"/"Alle akzeptieren") and French, missing Dutch/Italian/Spanish/Nordic/Polish equivalents despite the EU target market.

**Failure.** A German job board behind Sourcepoint renders a full-screen consent overlay with the content blurred behind it. `_dismiss_overlays` finds nothing (wrong frame, and too early), `_wait_for_job_content` times out after 6 s because no JobPosting marker is visible, and `page.content()` returns the consent wall. `universal_extract` pulls the consent dialog's heading as the job title.

**Fix.** Iterate `page.frames` (or use `page.frame_locator(...)`) for every selector; call dismissal *after* a short settle wait and again after `_wait_for_job_content`; add `Zustimmen`, `Einverstanden`, `Alle Cookies akzeptieren`, `Accepteren`, `Accetta`, `Aceptar`, `Godkänn`, `Godta`, `Accepter`, `Zgadzam się`. Consider seeding consent cookies per host instead of clicking.

### Crawler dedupes on raw URL strings with no canonicalisation, so tracking parameters and trailing slashes are crawl traps

`src/job_scraper/crawl.py:257` · perf

**What happens.** visited/queued hold the exact absolute URL (only the fragment is stripped at line 280). normalize.canonicalize_url exists and is used by every adapter, but the crawler never calls it. Faceted navigation, session/tracking parameters and /x vs /x/ therefore generate unbounded distinct URLs that all resolve to the same page.

**Failure.** A careers site with '?source=' or '?page=' links on every card exhausts the page budget re-fetching one page under N query strings and never reaches the job detail pages.

**Fix.** Key visited/queued on canonicalize_url(link) while still fetching the original, and add a per-path-prefix cap so one template cannot consume the whole budget.

### PlaywrightFetcher bypasses HttpClient entirely - no proxy, no robots, no throttle, no cache - and counts a failed navigation as a fetched page

`src/job_scraper/crawl.py:145` · anti-bot

**What happens.** PlaywrightFetcher.__enter__ launches chromium with no proxy= argument and the fetcher is used instead of self.http at crawl.py:239. Everything the proxy pool, per-host throttle and robots gate provide is skipped for the escalation path - the one path most likely to be used against a site that already blocked the plain client. Separately, page.goto is wrapped in suppress(Exception) (line 150-152), so a navigation timeout still falls through to `return page.url, page.content()`, handing the crawler an about:blank page that increments `pages` and is yielded as a real result.

**Failure.** On an employee machine whose IP is blocked (the reported production condition), the plain pass fails, the Playwright escalation runs from the same blocked IP, every goto times out, and each timeout is counted as a successfully crawled page - so max_pages is consumed by about:blank and the run reports pages crawled with zero listings.

**Fix.** Pass the pool's current proxy into chromium.launch(proxy={...}) and share the throttle; and only return content when goto succeeded and page.url is not about:blank, otherwise return None so the crawler skips it.

### Playwright escalation only runs the JSON-LD extractor, skipping universal_extract and mine_contacts

`src/job_scraper/deep_scrape.py:229` · correctness

**What happens.** The end-of-retries escalation path calls `extract_job_from_page(html, final_url)` and nothing else. The normal path (lines 165-187) runs three passes: JSON-LD, then `universal_extract` (which handles og:title/h1/title, fact-cards, company/location heuristics), then `mine_contacts` (recruiter name/phone/email — the highest-value lead fields). A page that only became readable via Playwright therefore yields nothing unless it happens to carry JSON-LD, and never yields contacts.

**Failure.** A JS-only SPA posting (jobbnorge.no, webcruiter) fails three plain HTTP attempts, escalates to Playwright, renders correctly — and because the SPA emits no JSON-LD, `extracted.title` is empty, the function returns `(False, ...)`, the listing stays a bare stub and is counted as a deep-scrape failure even though the HTML was in hand.

**Fix.** Factor lines 143-208 into a `_extract_all(html, final_url, listing, hint) -> bool` and call it from both the normal path and the escalation path, so the escalation gets the same three passes plus the LLM fallback.

### Deep-scrape retries re-read the identical cached body, so every extraction miss pays 1-8 s of pointless backoff

`src/job_scraper/deep_scrape.py:218` · perf

**What happens.** The retry loop treats `no-content` (fetch succeeded, extraction produced nothing) the same as `http-fail`. But `http.get` consults the SQLite cache first (`cache_enabled` defaults to True, TTL 86400), so attempts 2 and 3 are served the byte-identical body that already failed to extract, and are guaranteed to fail identically — after sleeping `uniform(1,2)` then `uniform(2,4)` seconds while holding a worker slot.

**Failure.** A 5,000-listing run with a 30% extraction-miss rate: 1,500 listings each sleep ~4.5 s across two useless retries = ~110 worker-minutes wasted, ~9 minutes of wall clock at `deep_concurrency: 12` — for zero additional data. With `deep_max_retries: 3` (as `norway-top5.yaml` sets) it is worse.

**Fix.** Only retry when the failure is transport-level (`http-fail`/`empty-body`). On `no-content`, either break immediately or retry once with `use_cache=False` so the second attempt can actually differ.

### `allow_404=True` plus the og:title veto lets expired/404 job pages be merged as successful enrichment

`src/job_scraper/deep_scrape.py:137` · data-quality

**What happens.** Deep-scrape calls `http.get(url, allow_404=True)`, which bypasses `if response.status_code >= 400 and not allow_404: return None` and hands the error body to the extractors. The only remaining guard is `_looks_like_block`, and its `_CONTENT_MARKERS` veto returns False as soon as the body contains `og:title` — which every branded 404 page has. `universal_extract` then takes og:title → h1 → `<title>` as the job title, `listing.merge` overwrites `title`/`company` whenever the new string is longer (they are not in `_FIRST_WINS_FIELDS`), and `_scrape_one` returns `(True, "ok-universal")`.

**Failure.** A posting is taken down between the listing crawl and the deep-scrape pass (routine — it is why lead runs are re-run). The host serves a 404 page with `og:title: "Seite nicht gefunden | HelloFresh"`. That string is longer than the real title "Data Analyst", so it overwrites it; the row is exported as a job titled "Seite nicht gefunden" at company "HelloFresh" and counted in the "OK" tally. Sales contacts a dead posting.

**Fix.** Have `http.get` return the status code alongside the body (or expose `last_status`), and in `_scrape_one` treat any `status >= 400` as a failure for enrichment purposes. At minimum, skip the merge when the extracted title matches a not-found pattern, and stop vetoing the block heuristic on `og:title` alone.

### Sitemap job-URL filter is English/German-only and requires a trailing slash, dropping most EU postings

`src/job_scraper/discovery.py:154` · coverage

**What happens.** `filter_job_urls` keeps a URL only if `/(jobs?|careers?|stellen|stelle|positions?|opening|vacanc|stellenangebote)/` matches. The pattern requires the token to be bounded by slashes on *both* sides, so any URL ending in the segment (`https://x.com/careers`, `https://x.com/en/jobs`) is dropped.
