Worse, when country_of returns '' (very common given the geo gaps above) the national TLD is never prepended for ANY country, so .com is tried first — the exact failure the COUNTRY_TLD comment says it exists to prevent (Empa -> empa.com, Suva -> suva.de).

**Failure.** A Bucharest company seeded from a Greenhouse board with location 'Bucharest' resolves country RO. COUNTRY_TLD has no RO entry, so tlds = DOMAIN_TLDS, .ro is never tried, and acme.ro is never discovered. If acme.com happens to resolve and answer 200 it is accepted instead — a different company entirely, whose staff are then exported under this employer's name.

**Fix.** Complete COUNTRY_TLD for every code in EU_COUNTRIES | EFTA_AND_UK (and add .uk alongside .co.uk for GB), and append the missing national TLDs to DOMAIN_TLDS. When the country is unknown, prefer a national TLD inferred from the seed's own market before falling back to .com, and never accept a .com guess without the company-name corroboration described in the resolve_domain finding.

### _LEGAL_SUFFIXES strips legal-form words from the start of a company name, mangling the stem

`src/leadgen/seed/atsboards.py:144` · correctness

**What happens.** The stripping regex is rf"(?:^|\s){suffix}(?=\s|$)", which matches a legal-form token anywhere in the name including the first word, and the loop runs up to three times. Legal-form abbreviations are also ordinary words and ordinary name components, so a company whose name begins with one loses it. The resulting stem is a different, usually real, company's name, and since guess_domains only filters candidates by DNS resolution, a short generic stem is very likely to resolve to a stranger's site and then be accepted by resolve_domain (which does no name check).

**Failure.** Verified: 'AG Solutions GmbH' -> trading name 'solutions' -> stem 'solutions'; 'Ab Initio Software' -> 'initio software'; 'Holding Graf KG' -> 'graf'; 'SE Group' -> '' (empty trading name). guess_domains then probes solutions.com and graf.de — both real sites belonging to other companies — and resolve_domain accepts whichever answers 200.

**Fix.** Only strip a legal-form token when it is not the first token of the name, and require at least one remaining token; if stripping would empty the name, keep the original. Keep the multi-word suffixes ('GmbH & Co. KG') matched anywhere since those are unambiguous.

### fetch_boards walks 1,157 boards serially with full job descriptions and no geography pre-filter, downloading ~640 MB to keep ~113 EU companies

`src/leadgen/seed/atsboards.py:294` · perf

**What happens.** `fetch_boards` is a plain `for kind, slug in boards:` loop with no concurrency, and `GREENHOUSE_URL` requests `content=true`, which inlines every job's full HTML description. Geography is only applied afterwards, at company level in `companies_from_listings`, so the entire US-dominated list is fetched before anything is discarded. There is no `--boards-countries` option and nothing in the boards file marks a slug's country.

**Failure.** The runbook's section-2 full run (`--boards configs/leads/boards.txt --countries eu`) spends 20-40 minutes and ~640 MB of transfer, 79% of it on postings that the country filter will discard minutes later.

**Fix.** Fetch boards through a ThreadPoolExecutor like the other seeds, drop `content=true` for the geography-scouting pass (or fetch descriptions only for boards that survive the country filter), and annotate boards.txt with a country column so a `--countries` run can skip slugs it will discard.

### guess_domains exhausts all 16 TLDs of the first stem before trying the trading name, so a bad slug blocks the good stem

`src/leadgen/seed/atsboards.py:176` · correctness

**What happens.** The candidate loop is `for stem in stems:` outer, `for tld in tlds:` inner, with `if len(live) >= MAX_GUESSES: return live`. Stems are ordered slug, trading-name, raw company. Since MAX_GUESSES is 4 and most single-word stems resolve on .com/.io/.co/.eu (parked or squatted), the function returns four candidates built from the *slug* and never evaluates the trading name at all. `resolve_domain` then accepts the first candidate that answers HTTP, with no check that the page belongs to the company.

**Failure.** Personio board slug `building-radar` with subcompany name 'Building Radar GmbH': the slug stem yields building-radar.com/.de/.eu/.io before the trading-name stem 'building radar' is ever tried. Where the slug is a campaign name rather than the brand (creatorsworkhere/Adorama), the guesses are all for a domain that is not the company's.

**Fix.** Interleave stems (try each stem's national + .com before moving to the next TLD tier), or raise MAX_GUESSES and let `resolve_domain` do the discriminating - and add a name-corroboration check there before accepting a guessed domain.

### Two countries in the shipped --countries europe set return zero for every keyword, so the 'probably rate-limiting this IP' warning fires on every clean run

`src/leadgen/seed/jobsearch.py:142` · silent-failure

**What happens.** `search_all` invokes `search_workable` once per country, so `pairs` equals the keyword count (52) and the warning triggers whenever more than half of one country's keywords return nothing. Iceland and Liechtenstein are both in `EFTA_AND_UK` and both have essentially no Workable inventory, so all 52 of their pairings come back empty on a perfectly healthy run and the warning fires with a diagnosis that is simply wrong. A warning that cries wolf every single run is one the operator learns to skip, which is exactly when a genuine block goes unnoticed.

**Failure.** Every REGION=europe run emits 'workable returned nothing for 52 of 52 queries - the host is probably rate-limiting this IP' twice, for Iceland and Liechtenstein. On the day Workable actually blocks the run, the same line for Poland and Spain looks like the usual noise.

**Fix.** Base the warning on the blocked-vs-empty distinction described above (payload is None) rather than on emptiness, and suppress it for countries whose page-1 `totalSize` was legitimately 0.

### SmartRecruiters' 'geography rides on the language of the keyword' premise is false for Romance languages, wasting the hard 98-row budget on out-of-scope rows

`src/leadgen/seed/jobsearch.py:176` · correctness

**What happens.** The module docstring asserts that a native-language term selects that market's employers. Measured live, that holds for German but collapses for Italian: the endpoint does fuzzy/semantic matching, not literal term matching. Because the endpoint hands back at most ~98 rows per keyword and cannot be paginated (see the pagination finding), every out-of-market row consumes budget that a geography-filtered call would have spent on an EU employer. Those rows are then discarded by `companies_from_listings(countries=...)` anyway.

**Failure.** The Italian block of configs/leads/keywords-multilingual.txt burns one request per term and returns ~4% Italian rows. The country filter then discards ~94 of 98 rows per Italian keyword, so those keywords contribute almost nothing while counting fully against the per-host request budget.

**Fix.** Move to the official postings API, which accepts `country=<iso2>` (verified live: `?limit=10&country=de` -> totalFound 121), so geography is a server-side parameter rather than a property of the query language. Failing that, drop the Romance-language keyword blocks and document that the trick only works for German.

### SmartRecruiters listings carry no description, no company website and no usable domain stem, so that seed's companies drop out at the highest rate of any source

`src/leadgen/seed/jobsearch.py:152` · coverage

**What happens.** `_smartrecruiters_listing` sets no `description` and no `company_website`. Downstream that means: (1) `contacts_from_ad` is called on an empty string in jobboard.py, so SmartRecruiters never yields an ad-mined contact or an email anchor - which is the stated value of an ad seed; (2) the only hints reaching `resolve_domain` are `apply_url`s on `jobs.smartrecruiters.com`, which `is_company_site` rejects as an ATS host; (3) `_ats_slug` in jobboard.py has no SmartRecruiters pattern, so `guess_domains` falls back to the display name. Meanwhile `company.identifier` - a clean board slug that would be an excellent domain stem - is read at line 154 and then discarded in favour of `company.name`.

**Failure.** A SmartRecruiters keyword returns 98 rows across ~60 employers. None has a description to mine, none has a stated website, and each is domain-guessed from its display name. The ones whose brand name is not their domain are dropped; the ones whose guess resolves to a parked or unrelated site are crawled under the wrong identity.

**Fix.** Carry `company.identifier` through (e.g. as an extra or as the slug argument), add a `jobs.smartrecruiters.com/([A-Za-z0-9-]+)/` pattern to `_SLUG_FROM_URL`, and fetch the per-posting detail (or the company's postings API entry) for the description when SmartRecruiters is enabled.

### Test coverage for these four modules exercises only the ASCII/DACH happy path

`tests/leadgen/test_pattern.py:19` · coverage

**What happens.** This is why 453 green tests coexist with the defects above. test_pattern.py tests exactly one accented case ('Jörg Müller' -> joerg.mueller), which is the one convention the hardcoded table gets right, and has no case for Nordic, Polish, hyphenated, multi-part-given-name, mononym, initial-form or foreign-domain anchors. test_geo.py asserts that 'Birmingham' is absent but never checks Manchester, London, Athens or Naples, and has no NFD case. test_domain.py is 69 lines with no parked-page, career-subdomain, non-denylisted-board or 403 case, so the single highest-impact behaviour in that module — what happens when the homepage returns a bot wall — is untested. Nothing asserts what email_status a blind guess receives.

**Failure.** Any of the fixes above can be made and the suite stays green either way, and any regression in these paths ships unnoticed. Concretely: the suite passes today while infer_pattern returns ('','') for every Swedish, Finnish, Polish and hyphenated-surname anchor.

**Fix.** Add table-driven tests over a realistic European name corpus (DE/AT/CH, FR, ES/PT, IT, NL, SE/FI/NO/DK, PL, CZ, GR) asserting both the generated address and round-trip inference from the real published form. Add geo tests for each colliding city with a US/CA qualifier, and an NFD case. Add domain tests for a 403 homepage, a career subdomain, a parked page and a non-denylisted regional board. Add an assemble test pinning email_status for the no-anchor path.


## LOW

### ONBOARDING names a CSV column that does not exist and claims a category typo falls back safely when it silently narrows

`docs/ONBOARDING.md:188` · docs-mismatch

**What happens.** Two smaller doc/code mismatches in the operator-facing column and parameter tables. (1) ONBOARDING.md:188 documents a column named `evidence`; the CSV header is `evidence_json` — src/leadgen/models.py:56 `LEAD_CSV_COLUMNS = ["id"] + LEAD_FIELDS + ["evidence_json"]` and models.py:106 writes `row["evidence_json"]`. docs/RUNBOOK-leads.md:114 and tools/verify_leads.py:57 both use the correct name. (The "26 columns" claim at :178 is correct: 1 + 24 + 1.) (2) ONBOARDING.md:310 states "`-Categories 106` narrows to pure IT. A typo falls back to the shipped four rather than silently widening to the whole board." That only holds when *every* id is malformed. src/leadgen/cli.py:87-90 `_int_list` returns `found or fallback`, so a partial typo keeps the digits it could parse and drops the rest — and tests/leadgen/test_daily.py:165 pins exactly that: `_int_list("106,oops,146", (1,)) == (106, 146)`. The test is right; the doc is wrong.

**Failure.** An operator writes a spreadsheet formula against a column called `evidence` and gets #REF on every row. Separately, an operator typing `-Categories "106,1456,156,167"` (transposed digits) gets a run over three valid categories plus one nonexistent id, silently narrowing the sector rather than falling back to the shipped four as promised — and nothing in the output says the filter differed from what they asked for.

**Fix.** Rename the doc entry to `evidence_json`. Rewrite :310 to say that unparseable ids are dropped individually and only a fully unparseable list falls back — or make `_int_list` reject the whole list when any element fails to parse, which matches the documented promise.

### OPERATOR-TERMS' end-of-access deletion list omits logs/, which contains the same personal data as the CSVs

`docs/OPERATOR-TERMS.md:76` · security

**What happens.** Line 74-77 ("## What to do on your last day") instructs: "Delete the repository folder, the `.cache/` directory, the `output/` directory, and every list you exported anywhere else." It omits `logs/`, which scripts/daily.sh:74 and scripts/daily.ps1:83-86 create and into which daily.sh:150 / daily.ps1:164 tee the complete run output. That log carries INFO-level lines including `register: %s -> %d people on the public record` with company names (src/leadgen/person/register.py:368), the funnel breakdown, and every seed URL; at `-v` it carries DEBUG lines naming rejected person names (src/leadgen/assemble.py:142). The `.cache/` entry is listed but the checkpoint SQLite inside it holds the full serialised Lead rows (src/leadgen/checkpoint.py:28-31, `leads(id, payload)`) — that one is at least covered by the folder deletion.

**Failure.** An operator's access ends. They follow the checklist precisely and confirm to BluOryn that they have deleted everything. `logs/run-ch-2026-08-23.log` and every other daily log remains on their personal laptop, containing company names, funnel data and — at verbose level — candidate person names, for a dataset the same document calls personal data under GDPR.

**Fix.** Add `logs/` to the deletion list. Consider having daily.sh/daily.ps1 write logs into a directory covered by the same retention rule, or redact person names from INFO-level output.

### The runbook's Swiss re-cut points at a checkpoint the documented Swiss harvest never writes

`docs/RUNBOOK-leads.md:220` · docs-mismatch

**What happens.** The Swiss "Run it" command passes no `--checkpoint`, so it writes to the CLI default `.cache/leadgen.sqlite` (cli.py:191). The two commands that follow — "Getting every name, not just the target families" and "The commercial register" — pass `--checkpoint .cache/swiss.sqlite`. They read a different, empty database. The default also means the EU run and the Swiss run share one checkpoint, which scripts/daily.sh:76-78 explicitly warns against ("Sharing one would let a Swiss run mark a German employer as already crawled").

**Failure.** An operator follows the runbook literally: runs the 35-page Swiss harvest (banking ~2,270 people into .cache/leadgen.sqlite), then runs the documented `--select-only --roles any --target 2000 --checkpoint .cache/swiss.sqlite` re-cut that the runbook says "produced 2,000 rows". It opens an empty database, writes a header-only CSV, prints SHORTFALL 2000 and exits 2. The operator concludes the harvest failed.

**Fix.** Add `--checkpoint .cache/swiss.sqlite` to the Swiss "Run it" command so all three Swiss commands agree, and make the CLI's default checkpoint config-derived (e.g. `.cache/leadgen-<config stem>.sqlite`) so two different products cannot share one ledger by accident.

### NEXT_TEXT_RX is defined and never used, so multi-word Next labels ('Nächste Seite', 'More results') are not matched

`extension/app/src/content/paginator.ts:12` · correctness

**What happens.** NEXT_TEXT_RX is a substring pattern clearly intended as the looser fallback for Next-button text. It has no reference anywhere in the codebase. Only NEXT_RX — fully anchored with ^...$ — is used, so a control labelled 'Nächste Seite' or 'Weiter zu Seite 3' does not match. tsconfig has strict:false and no noUnusedLocals, so the dead constant does not surface as an error.

**Failure.** A German board renders `<a>Nächste Seite</a>` outside any element matching PAGINATION_SEL. NEXT_RX does not match the two-word label, findNextButton returns null, and detectPagination falls through to the infinite-scroll false positive instead of following the real link.

**Fix.** Use NEXT_TEXT_RX as the second-chance test in findNextButton (after NEXT_RX, with the existing text.length < 20 guard), or delete it. Turn on noUnusedLocals in tsconfig so the next dead constant is caught at typecheck time.

### csvCell does not flatten a lone carriage return, defeating its own stated purpose

`extension/app/src/lib/export.ts:32` · correctness

**What happens.** The replace is /\r?\n/g, which matches LF and CRLF but leaves a bare CR untouched. The comment says 'flatten newlines so a description can't break the row'. Since the file itself is joined with CRLF, a stray CR inside a quoted field is a row separator for several lenient CSV readers and for older Excel versions.

**Failure.** A description scraped from a page that used classic-Mac line endings (or a <pre> block containing \r) keeps its bare CRs. The exported row splits across lines in a downstream importer and the remaining 40-odd columns shift, silently corrupting every field after description for that row.

**Fix.** Use .replace(/[\r\n]+/g, " ") (and consider stripping \t and other C0 controls at the same time).

### Every export is base64-encoded into a data: URL, and the library is loaded whole into memory on every change

`extension/app/src/lib/export.ts:54` · perf

**What happens.** downloadBlob reads the entire Blob through FileReader.readAsDataURL and hands the resulting base64 string to chrome.downloads.download, inflating the payload by ~33% and holding both the CSV string and its base64 form in memory at once. Separately, Library.tsx:20 does useLiveQuery(() => db.jobs.toArray()) — the whole table, including descriptions capped at 10,000 chars and raw_jsonld capped at 8,000 chars, is materialised in the side panel on every DB change, and every crawl write triggers a re-read.

**Failure.** 50,000 rows averaging 6 KB is a ~300 MB CSV; base64 pushes the data: URL past 400 MB and the export either OOMs the side panel or is rejected by the downloads API. Long before that, the live query re-materialises 300 MB on every single crawl write, so the side panel becomes unusable during a run.

**Fix.** Build the CSV in chunks and use URL.createObjectURL(blob) from the side panel (a document context, where blob URLs work fine and the data:-URL workaround is unnecessary), revoking after the download starts. Replace toArray() with a paged/indexed Dexie query driven by the virtualizer's visible range, and exclude description/raw_jsonld from the list query.

### Unknown adapter names and a discovery hint with no adapter both fail silently into GenericAdapter

`src/job_scraper/adapters/__init__.py:51` · silent-failure

**What happens.** get_adapter does `ADAPTERS.get(adapter_name) or ADAPTERS['generic']`, so a typo in a config's `adapter:` key becomes a full generic crawl of the site rather than an error. Relatedly, discovery.py:32 maps jobvite hosts to the name 'jobvite', which is not a key in ADAPTERS - generic.py:28-29 gets None and `continue`s, so every Jobvite board discovered on a homepage is dropped without a word.

**Failure.** A config saying `adapter: greenhosue` quietly BFS-crawls boards.greenhouse.io as an unknown website; an operator reading configs/ believes Jobvite is supported because it is listed in ATS_HOST_PATTERNS.

**Fix.** Raise (or log an error naming the valid keys) when an explicitly configured adapter name is unknown, and either implement a jobvite adapter or remove it from ATS_HOST_PATTERNS.

### Every EU adapter appends a duplicate page parameter when the target URL already contains one

`src/job_scraper/adapters/jobsch.py:67` · correctness

**What happens.** jobsch.py:67 and jobbsafari.py:38 build `f"{base}{sep}page={page}"` without inspecting `base`; finn.py:58, nav.py:49 and karrierestart.py:38 append `("page", str(page))` to a parameter list that was parsed straight out of the user's URL. In every case a pre-existing `page=` in the configured URL survives and a second one is appended.

**Failure.** An operator copies a paginated search URL out of the browser — e.g. `https://www.jobs.ch/en/vacancies/?term=python&page=3` — into a config. The adapter emits `...&page=3&page=2`, `...&page=3&page=3`, and so on. Which value the server honours is undefined; jobs.ch resolves it to page 3 every time, so the loop sees `new == 0` on iteration two and returns a single page. Silent, and a very natural thing for a user to do.

**Fix.** Strip `page` (and `rows`/`from`) from the inbound query string before building each request, in all five adapters.

### posted_date is never parsed or normalised — four different formats land in one CSV column

`src/job_scraper/adapters/jobsch.py:157` · data-quality

**What happens.** No module in src/job_scraper parses `posted_date`; every adapter assigns whatever the remote gives it (`grep -rn 'fromisoformat|dateutil|_parse_date' src/job_scraper/*.py` returns nothing). The five European adapters each supply a different shape, and two of them mix a UTC instant with a local-time date.

**Failure.** Any recency analysis on the export has to handle four formats. A jobs.ch posting stamped 2026-09-21T00:30+02:00 is 2026-09-20T22:30Z; truncating the two representations to a date puts the same posting on two different days depending on which adapter produced it. And NAV rows carry a last-modified date, so an ad edited today looks like it was posted today.

**Fix.** Normalise `posted_date` to a single representation (UTC ISO-8601, or a plain date consistently derived in the source's local timezone) in one place — a `_normalize_dates` step applied after merge — and prefer NAV's adData `published` field over the 'Sist endret' label for the publication date.

### karrierestart detail pages redirect to a different host than the one recorded as source_domain

`src/job_scraper/adapters/karrierestart.py:56` · correctness

**What happens.** The adapter stamps `source_domain="karrierestart.no"` on every listing, but a share of detail pages 301 to the `hyre.karrierestart.no` subdomain. That is a distinct origin for robots.txt, for the per-host throttle bucket in `HostThrottle` (keyed on `urlparse(url).netloc`), and for the per-proxy cookie jar — so the politeness budget the operator configured for karrierestart.no is applied to two separate buckets, at double the intended rate.

**Failure.** With `deep_per_host_delay_seconds: 0.4` configured for karrierestart, the effective request rate against the karrierestart infrastructure is up to 2x the configured limit because half the traffic lands on a second host key. On a site already returning nothing but cookie banners, that is the fastest route to an IP ban.

**Fix.** Record `source_domain` from the post-redirect `final_url`, and key the throttle on the registrable domain (eTLD+1) rather than the full netloc so subdomains of one site share one politeness budget.

### Personio fabricates a board host from an arbitrary company hostname

`src/job_scraper/adapters/personio.py:42` · correctness

**What happens.** When the URL is not a *.jobs.personio.{de,com} host, _resolve_bases takes host.split('.')[0] as the slug. For a normal company URL that first label is usually 'www', producing https://www.jobs.personio.de. This path is reachable whenever a config pins `adapter: personio` on a company URL.

**Failure.** Two requests to a nonexistent Personio subdomain, each answered with 429 + marketing HTML (see the Personio 429 finding), costing 16s of sleep and a proxy failure mark, for a company that is not on Personio at all.

**Fix.** Return [] when the host is not a Personio board host, or require the caller to pass an explicit slug.

### Personio feed detection rejects a BOM-prefixed XML document and ignores additionalOffices

`src/job_scraper/adapters/personio.py:51` · correctness

**What happens.** `body.strip().startswith('<?xml')` - str.strip() does not remove U+FEFF, so a feed served with a UTF-8 BOM is discarded as 'not XML' even though safe_xml would parse it. discovery.probe_personio (discovery.py:168) has the same guard. Separately the parser reads <office> but not <additionalOffices>/<office>, dropping secondary locations.

**Failure.** A Personio tenant whose feed carries a BOM yields 0 jobs with no log line; a Munich+Berlin posting is filed as Munich only, so a Berlin-scoped run misses it.

**Fix.** Use body.lstrip('﻿ \t\r\n').startswith('<?xml') (or just hand it to safe_xml and check the root tag), and join office with additionalOffices/office.

### Workable and SmartRecruiters return 'www' as the board slug for www.* hosts

`src/job_scraper/adapters/workable.py:111` · correctness

**What happens.** workable._extract_slug returns host.split('.')[0] for any *.workable.com host other than apply.workable.com, so www.workable.com/acme yields 'www'. smartrecruiters._extract_slug does the same at line 110-112 for a path-less host. Both then make live API calls for a board named 'www'.

**Failure.** A discovered link to the vendor's own marketing site (extremely common on careers pages - discover_from_homepage matches `\.workable\.com`) is delegated as a board named 'www', wasting requests and logging nothing.

**Fix.** Reject reserved subdomains ('www','apply','jobs','careers','api') as slugs and fall back to the path segment or give up.

### Export credentials live in tracked YAML with no environment-variable indirection

`src/job_scraper/config.py:103` · security

**What happens.** `NotionExportConfig.token`, `SlackExportConfig.webhook_url`, `GSheetsExportConfig.service_account_json` and `RunConfig.llm_api_key` are plain string fields read straight from YAML, and `configs/` is a tracked directory. Only `llm_api_key` has an env fallback, and only inside `llm_adapter` (`api_key or os.environ.get("ANTHROPIC_API_KEY")`) — the config loader itself performs no `${VAR}` expansion anywhere. `.gitignore` protects only `*.local.yaml` / `*.private.yaml` / `configs/local/`, which nothing in the loader or the docs steers the operator toward for a normal region config.

**Failure.** An operator fills in `token:` and `webhook_url:` in `configs/regions/germany.yaml` (a tracked file, as the example config demonstrates) and commits. The Notion integration token and Slack webhook are now in git history and in every clone on every employee device.

**Fix.** Expand `${ENV_VAR}` (and `${ENV_VAR:-default}`) in string values during `load_config`, document env vars as the supported way to supply credentials, and refuse to start (or warn loudly) when a non-empty secret is found in a file under a git-tracked path.

### Notion export keeps posting one page per job after an auth failure instead of aborting

`src/job_scraper/export/notion_exporter.py:33` · perf

**What happens.** `export_notion` iterates every job, calling `_post_with_retry` and then unconditionally `time.sleep(0.35)`. `_post_with_retry` returns False on 401/403/400 and logs a warning per job, but the caller has no abort condition — a permanently bad token produces N failed requests, N warning lines, and N × 0.35 s of sleeping.

**Failure.** The Notion integration token is rotated. A 5,000-row export spends ~30 minutes issuing 5,000 doomed POSTs and writes 5,000 identical `notion: HTTP 401` warnings to the log before reporting `0 pages created, 5000 failed`.

**Fix.** Make `_post_with_retry` distinguish fatal (401/403/404, and 400 on the first row = schema mismatch) from per-row failures, and break out of the loop with a single clear error when the first fatal status arrives. Skip the trailing sleep after the final job.

### _collect_section_body does not stop at the apply CTA, so 'Jetzt bewerben' is appended to requirements

`src/job_scraper/extract.py:464` · data-quality

**What happens.** _collect_section_body walks forward from a heading collecting every sibling's <li>/<p> text until _starts_new_section fires. A trailing apply link or CTA paragraph that is not wrapped in a heading is collected into the last section body.

**Failure.** Every posting whose description ends with an inline apply link has that CTA text glued onto the last parsed section, so responsibilities/requirements/qualifications all end with '• Jetzt bewerben'.

**Fix.** Skip siblings whose entire text matches APPLY_TEXT_RX or that contain only an anchor, and stop collecting at a sibling that is (or wraps) a lone link.

### canonicalize_url and the extension's canonicalizeUrl diverge on valueless query parameters

`src/job_scraper/models.py:44` · correctness

**What happens.** Python uses parse_qsl(keep_blank_values=True) + urlencode, which rewrites a valueless parameter '?foo' as 'foo='. The extension mutates URLSearchParams in place and preserves '?foo'. Both files carry comments asserting byte-identical behaviour because both write the same `id` column.

**Failure.** A posting reachable at https://acme.de/jobs/1?preview canonicalises to '...?preview=' in the CLI and '...?preview' in the extension, producing two different SHA-1 ids for the same posting and defeating cross-half dedupe.

**Fix.** Preserve the original key-only form on the Python side (emit the bare key when the parsed value is empty and the raw query had no '='), or normalise both sides to always append '='.

### safe_xml raises TypeError on bytes input when defusedxml is absent, and refuses legitimate documents that declare a DOCTYPE

`src/job_scraper/safe_xml.py:59` · correctness

**What happens.** The security core of this module is sound - I verified that with defusedxml 0.7.1 installed (it is, and it is a hard dependency in pyproject.toml) both billion-laughs and an external-entity XXE return None, and that malformed input returns None rather than raising. Two rough edges remain on the no-defusedxml fallback path. First, `_DOCTYPE_RX.search(text)` is a str pattern, so bytes input raises an uncaught `TypeError: cannot use a string pattern on a bytes-like object` - the defused path accepts bytes happily, so the two paths have different signatures and the fallback would crash a run that the primary path survives. Second, the fallback refuses any document containing `<!DOCTYPE`, which includes real sitemaps that carry a harmless `<!DOCTYPE urlset>` and any XHTML error page; those are dropped rather than parsed. Also, `MAX_XML_BYTES` counts str characters, not bytes, so a UTF-8 document can be up to ~4x the nominal cap, and the cap is applied only after the whole body is already resident in memory (HttpClient has no response size limit at all, so `response.text` on a multi-GB body OOMs before safe_xml is reached).

**Failure.** An operator installs from requirements.txt into an environment where defusedxml fails to build (or uses the extension-only subset), then a sitemap carrying `<!DOCTYPE urlset>` is fetched: safe_xml returns None and the whole site's sitemap is discarded silently. If any caller is later changed to pass `response.content` instead of `response.text`, the same path raises TypeError up through `_urls_in` and aborts the company.

**Fix.** Accept `str | bytes` explicitly - decode bytes (or use a bytes pattern) before the regex scan. Make defusedxml a hard import failure rather than a silent degradation, or narrow the fallback to reject only `<!ENTITY` and DOCTYPEs with an internal subset. Measure the cap in bytes, and add a streaming size limit in HttpClient.get so an oversized body never materialises.

### source_email_url is a URL column that frequently contains a non-URL sentinel

`src/leadgen/assemble.py:201` · data-quality

**What happens.** `_resolve_email` returns `f"inferred:{pattern}"` or `f"guessed:{FALLBACK_PATTERN}"` as its evidence_url (assemble.py:75, 79), and build_leads assigns that verbatim to `lead.source_email_url` as well as into evidence_json. The runbook documents the `inferred:` convention for evidence_json only; source_email_url sits alongside source_seed_url and source_person_url, which are real URLs, and the buyer's tooling will treat it as one.

**Failure.** On a run with --no-guess disabled (the default), the majority of rows carry source_email_url='guessed:first.last'. A buyer importing to a CRM that validates URL-typed columns either rejects those rows or renders them as a broken link. verify_leads.py's traceability check passes anyway because it only requires evidence_json to be non-empty and parseable.

**Fix.** Leave source_email_url empty for derived addresses (the email_status and email_confidence columns already carry that information honestly) and keep the `inferred:`/`guessed:` sentinel confined to evidence_json, where the runbook documents it.

### evidence_json carries provenance for only 3 of 24 fields, contradicting the runbook's GDPR justification

`src/leadgen/assemble.py:187` · docs-mismatch

**What happens.** `set_evidence` is called exactly three times in the codebase: person_name, person_role and person_email. Company-level fields (company_country, company_city, company_industry, tech_stack, company_website, company_size_hint) and person_linkedin/person_phone carry no provenance at all. docs/RUNBOOK-leads.md documents evidence_json as "Source URL per field" and, in the compliance note, rests the legitimate-interest argument on "every field carries its source URL in evidence_json".

**Failure.** A data subject exercises their Article 15 right of access and asks where the controller obtained their employer's country and industry classification. The delivered file has no answer for those fields, and the operator has been told in writing by the runbook that it does. The same gap means person_phone — arguably the most sensitive field in the row — is the one field with no recorded source.

**Fix.** Either record provenance for the remaining fields (the seed listing URL is available as company.seed_url for every company-level field, and hit.source_url covers linkedin/phone), or correct both the column description and the compliance note to state precisely which fields are traceable.

### cli logs 'skipping search seed' and then runs the search seed anyway

`src/leadgen/cli.py:334` · docs-mismatch

**What happens.** When `--search-keywords` is given without a geography, the code warns 'seed: --search-keywords needs --countries; skipping search seed' but does not skip anything - `search_all` is still called unconditionally a few lines later. With `countries=[]` the Workable half is a genuine no-op, but the SmartRecruiters half still runs if `--search-keywords-multilingual` was supplied, so the run does execute a search seed after announcing that it would not.

**Failure.** Operator runs with both keyword files but forgets `--countries`. The log says the search seed was skipped, yet 55 SmartRecruiters requests are made and their listings enter the funnel, so the seed counts the operator is reading do not match what ran.

**Fix.** Either actually skip (guard the `search_all` call), or reword the warning to say that only the Workable half is disabled and the multilingual half will still run.

### valid_syntax does not enforce the 64-octet local-part limit

`src/leadgen/email/validate.py:155` · correctness

**What happens.** The syntax check caps only the total address at 254 characters. RFC 5321 limits the local part to 64 octets, and virtually every MTA enforces it. German and Dutch compound surnames combined with a two-part given name routinely exceed it once _slug concatenates them (see the multi-part-name finding), so the pipeline mints addresses that are guaranteed to be rejected at RCPT and to count as a hard bounce against the sender's reputation.

**Failure.** Verified: valid_syntax accepts a 95-character local part. A name like 'Hans-Peter Schmidtke-Finkelsteinbergmann' rendered through first.last produces a local part over 64 octets, passes validation, is exported, and bounces on first send.

**Fix.** Add a local-part length check (<=64) and a domain length check (<=253) to valid_syntax, and have apply_pattern return "" rather than construct an over-long local part.

### net_guard ignores the port and the url-embedded credential form, and HttpClient.head sends no proxies

`src/leadgen/net_guard.py:68` · security

**What happens.** `is_safe_url` validates scheme and host but never the port, so a guarded URL may target any port on a public host (http://public.example:25/, :22, :6379). For a crawler whose URLs come from scraped data this is a usable outbound port-scanner / SMTP-relay probe. Separately, `HttpClient.head` (http.py:583) is the only request method that omits `proxies=`, so it would fall through to requests' environment-proxy handling and out over the real IP; it currently has no callers in src/, so this is latent rather than live. The `_ResponseCache` class docstring claims it is "Keyed by canonical URL" but `get`/`put` use the raw request URL (http.py:261 vs 276), so equivalent URLs miss the cache.

**Failure.** A hostile job ad supplies apply_url = http://mail-relay.partner.example:25/. `guard()` passes it (public host), and HttpClient opens a TCP connection to port 25 from the VPS - enough to be logged as an SMTP probe by the target and to get the VPS IP listed. Nothing in the run records that a non-HTTP port was dialled.

**Fix.** Reject any port outside {80, 443, 8080, 8443} (or an explicit allowlist) in is_safe_url. Pass `proxies=self._proxies_dict(...)` in head() and take a proxy entry from the pool the way get() does. Either canonicalise the cache key or correct the docstring.

### impressum's legal-entity regex false-rejects short real surnames

`src/leadgen/person/strategies/impressum.py:49` · correctness

**What happens.** `_ENTITY_RX` matches the bare tokens `ab`, `oy`, `as`, `sl`, `plc` and `se` under `\b` boundaries against the whole candidate name. Those are real words and real short surnames in the Benelux and Nordics: 'Ab Vermeulen' (Ab is a Dutch given name), 'Jan As' (As is a Norwegian surname), 'Bo Ab', 'Alexander Sl'. Any such person named on an Impressum is discarded as a company.

**Failure.** A Dutch colofon reads 'Bestuurder: Ab Vermeulen'. The only named person on the page is rejected as a legal entity and the company reports no_person_found.

**Fix.** Only treat the short ambiguous forms (ab, oy, as, se, sl) as legal-entity markers when they appear at the END of the candidate (where a legal form always sits) or are followed by a period, rather than anywhere in the string. Keep the unambiguous multi-letter forms (gmbh, sarl, ltd, llc) as free-floating matches.

### press.py leaves the employer glued onto the extracted role for German genitive and French elided forms

`src/leadgen/person/strategies/press.py:40` · data-quality

**What happens.** `_CONNECTOR_RX` lists `of|at|bei|von|chez|di|presso|en` as the connectors that introduce an employer after a title, but German press copy uses the genitive article ('Geschäftsführer der Acme GmbH', 'Leiter des Bereichs') and French uses elision ('Directeur Général d'Acme SA'), neither of which is in the list. `_trim_company` therefore returns the whole phrase and the employer name ends up in the exported `person_role` field. classify_role still resolves correctly, so this is cosmetic rather than a lost lead, but it is visible junk in the deliverable.

**Failure.** A German press release yields a row whose person_role column reads 'Geschäftsführer der Acme GmbH' instead of 'Geschäftsführer'. Any downstream mail-merge that renders the role verbatim produces 'Sehr geehrter Geschäftsführer der Acme GmbH Weiß'.

**Fix.** Add `der|des|dem|van|voor` and the elided forms `d'|l'|dell'|dall'` to `_CONNECTOR_RX` (the elided ones need no leading whitespace requirement, e.g. `\s+d[’']`). Keep the existing 'only trim when the remainder still classifies' guard so 'Head of HR' is not truncated.

### The 'unknown' email-status weight is unreachable and contradicts the declared status order

`src/leadgen/score/completeness.py:12` · correctness

**What happens.** EMAIL_STATUS_WEIGHTS gives 'unknown' 10.0, above 'inferred_low' at 8.0 — but models.py:19 declares EMAIL_STATUS_ORDER ("Best first. The quota scorer and the CSV consumer both rely on this order") with inferred_low *ahead* of unknown. Nothing in the codebase reads EMAIL_STATUS_ORDER except a test asserting its first and last element, so the two disagree with no guard. Separately, `_resolve_email` never emits 'unknown': a verdict of 'unknown' (smtp disabled, or the probe refused) falls through assemble.py:100-105 to inferred_low/high/medium, so the weight is dead code — while the runbook documents `unknown (probe refused)` as a status operators will see in the email_status column.

**Failure.** If anyone later makes assemble emit 'unknown' (the obvious change when --no-smtp is used), those rows would immediately outrank every inferred_low row in the cut, inverting the documented quality ladder — and no test would catch it, because test_score.py:41 actively asserts `catch_all < unknown` rather than checking against EMAIL_STATUS_ORDER.

**Fix.** Derive the weights from EMAIL_STATUS_ORDER (or assert at import that `sorted(EMAIL_STATUS_WEIGHTS, key=..., reverse=True) == EMAIL_STATUS_ORDER`, the same way models.py already asserts LEAD_FIELDS against the dataclass), and remove 'unknown' from the documented status ladder in docs/RUNBOOK-leads.md since it is never produced.

### seniority is scored but never populated, so one scoring dimension is unearnable and one CSV column is always empty

`src/leadgen/score/completeness.py:34` · data-quality

**What happens.** FIELD_WEIGHTS awards 1.0 for a populated `seniority`, but `build_leads` (assemble.py:166-186) never sets it and `CompanyContext` has no seniority field. The value exists on JobListing (job_scraper/models.py:179) and is read by seed/atsboards.py:279, but it dies at the listing-to-company grouping step and never reaches a Lead. The column is therefore blank in every delivered row.

**Failure.** Every row of every CSV has an empty `seniority` column, and no lead can ever earn that point. The buyer sees a promised column with no data; a downstream filter on seniority matches nothing. The same is worth checking for `company_region` and `company_size_hint`, which depend entirely on the upstream adapter populating JobListing.region/company_size.

**Fix.** Either carry seniority through — add it to CompanyContext (or better, to PersonHit, since seniority is a property of the person's title) and set it in build_leads — or drop `seniority` from LEAD_FIELDS and from FIELD_WEIGHTS so the schema stops promising it.

### The country ceiling relaxes to 100% and the report never says it was breached

`src/leadgen/score/quota.py:129` · data-quality

**What happens.** When every country with remaining supply is at the cap, `pool = under_cap or available` silently drops the ceiling. That relaxation is a deliberate documented design choice (quota.py docstring), but it is invisible in the output: QuotaReport has no field recording that the cap was exceeded, and `summary()` prints only the raw per-country counts. The operator reading a `--country-ceiling 0.25` run has no way to tell 'the spread held' from 'the cap was abandoned on the first pass'.

**Failure.** Verified: 100 DE leads, target=40, country_ceiling=0.25 (cap=10). The result is DE=40 — the cap was exceeded fourfold — and the printed summary reads `requested 40 · candidates 100 · selected 40 / countries: DE=40`, with no mention of the ceiling. tools/verify_leads.py only NOTEs concentration above 60%, and that is explicitly labelled "Not a failure".

**Fix.** Add `ceiling_relaxed: bool` (and the cap value) to QuotaReport, set it the first time `under_cap` is empty while `available` is not, and print a line in summary() such as `country ceiling relaxed: DE 40 > cap 10 — supply too concentrated to spread`.

### The IT title gate is applied to Workable listings whose query was already an IT keyword, discarding ~4% for no benefit

`src/leadgen/seed/jobboard.py:151` · coverage

**What happens.** cli.py already exempts jobs.ch from the title gate with `require_it_role=False`, on the stated reasoning that 'a seed whose query already fixed the sector has nothing to gain from re-deciding it from the job title'. The identical reasoning applies to the Workable and SmartRecruiters seeds, whose every row was returned *because* it matched an IT search term from keywords.txt - yet they go through `companies_from_listings(other, ...)` with the gate on. Separately, the pattern `programm(?:er|ier\w*|atore)` does not match the English gerund 'Programming'.

**Failure.** A company whose only open Workable role is titled 'Programming- (Various Disciplines) - Talent Pool (EU)' is dropped by the gate, even though the posting was returned by an explicit IT keyword search.

**Fix.** Split the non-jobsch listings by source and pass `require_it_role=False` for the keyword-search seeds (keeping it on for arbeitnow and the ATS boards, where it earns its keep). Add `ing` to the programming alternation.

### jobs.ch hardcodes country='CH' while the posting's own addressCountry is parsed and available two lines above

`src/leadgen/seed/jobsch.py:152` · correctness

**What happens.** `_listing_from_detail` reads the JSON-LD `address` dict for `addressLocality`, `addressRegion` and `postalCode`, then sets `country="CH"` unconditionally, ignoring `address["addressCountry"]`. jobs.ch does list cross-border roles, and where it does, the listing is mislabelled - which then feeds `guess_domains(..., country)` and puts `.ch` first for a German or Austrian employer, the exact mis-resolution that the COUNTRY_TLD table exists to prevent.

**Failure.** A German employer advertises a Freiburg role on jobs.ch. The listing is stamped CH, `guess_domains` tries `<name>.ch` before `<name>.de`, and a run with `--countries CH` counts it toward the Swiss quota.

**Fix.** `country=str(address.get("addressCountry") or "CH").strip().upper()[:2]`, handling the dict form (`{"@type":"Country","name":"CH"}`) that the company-profile Organization block uses.

### website_hints() is dead code, and its docstring implies a Workable optimisation that is never wired in

`src/leadgen/seed/jobsearch.py:186` · correctness

**What happens.** `website_hints` builds a company -> employer-website map and documents itself as the thing that lets 'the seed skip domain guessing'. Nothing in the pipeline calls it - only tests/leadgen/test_jobsearch.py imports it. The data happens to reach `resolve_domain` anyway via the `company_website` attribute in `jobboard.build`, so the effect is confusion rather than data loss, but a reader auditing why the Workable website advantage is not being realised is sent to a function that is never executed.

**Failure.** Not a runtime failure; a maintainer changes `website_hints` to improve domain coverage and observes no change in the funnel, because the function is not on any code path.

**Fix.** Either delete it, or call it from cli.py and pass the map into `companies_from_listings` so the Workable-supplied websites are ordered ahead of the ATS URLs explicitly rather than by attribute-iteration accident.
